#2857. That 21-year-old who was DQ'ed from Q-school? His father has thoughts

Simon Kennedy acknowledges his son screwed up. No one is disputing that. The smoking gun was right there on the 11th green on the Hills course at Infinitum resort in Spain, about 60 miles down the coast from Barcelona: damaged turf caused by the putter head of Simon's 21-year-old tour-pro son, Frank. The torn-up patch has since been repaired, but if you know where to look, you can still make out the outline of the byproduct of a golfer behaving badly.

The incident occurred Monday, in the fourth round of the DP World Tour's Qualifying School's six-round finale. Frank Kennedy, who was born in England but raised in the golf hotbed of Jupiter, Fla., arrived on the par-5 11th hole at 12 under for the tournament and in excellent position to make the 72-hole cut in a marathon event that whittles 156 hopefuls down to 15 DPWT card-earners. Kennedy hit the 11th green in regulation, missed what he felt was a makeable birdie putt and tapped in. As he was walking off the green, he did what many golfers do when blowing off steam: He drew back his putter like a polo stick, with the intent, he said, of swinging the club through the air.

One problem: Kennedy misjudged the putter's arc, he said, and instead of whooshing through space, the club gouged the green — which ultimately resulted in DPWT officials disqualifying Kennedy for a Code of Conduct violation. The extent of the damage from Kennedy's actions is unclear. Monday Q Info's Ryan French, who broke the story, reported that multiple sources told him that Kennedy "took 'a divot' out of the 11th green. One player described it as 'a one foot divot' about 4 feet behind the hole."

Kennedy, in a Monday statement, said that the damage was "accidental." On Tuesday, he issued another statement in which he characterized the mar as a "4-inch graze" and said "this will be my final statement on the matter."

Frank's father, though, had more to say. In a phone interview with GOLF on Tuesday morning, Simon, a veteran sports agent who does not represent his son but has worked with many high-profile soccer and cricket players, said he was not onsite in Spain but has spoken extensively to Frank about what happened. Simon said the putter head "skidded across the surface, which players do thousands of times. I watch enough golf to see it. Stupidly, the surface was wet, he was probably a millimeter off and he caught the turf. The damage it caused was not a foot [deep] trench or a divot — it was a 4-inch-long, 1.5-inch-wide track, a graze, not a divot."

Simon continued, "I'm not trying to mitigate the damage; the damage is the damage." Over the course of a 45-minute interview, Simon repeatedly stressed this point: He doesn't condone his son's "foolish" and "careless" actions, saying, "On a moral level, he deserves some sort of a talking to or punishment." But Simon, who said he has never known his son to be a hothead or "club slammer," also feels that anyone who is assessing or passing judgment on the incident should know that his son did not intend to damage the green. The episode, as Simon described it, was a flash of petulance, a deeply regrettable error from a young player, with an ugly but wholly unintentional result.

Playing alongside Frank were Santiago Tarrio, a 36-year-old Spaniard who twice has won on the DPWT's feeder circuit, the HotelPlanner Tour, and Ren Yoweza, a 27-year-old Japanese pro who largely plays on his home tour.

When Frank saw what he had done to the green, he was "shocked," Simon said. Simon said Frank turned to Tarrio and said, "'S—, what do I do here?' And Tarrio and himself — Tarrio watched as Frank went down to do his repairs, as he would do with a ball mark or anything like that. He did the best job he could do. And then he said, 'What do you think?' The common consensus was that he was OK."

Pro DQ'ed for 'serious misconduct' in pivotal Q-school round, issues rebuttal

Simon said he didn't know of anyone in Frank's group who had witnessed the putter swipe, as Frank was the last player to exit the green, with the other players and caddies ahead of him. Even Frank's caddie, Gary Tilston, didn't even know about the incident until after the round, Simon said. "Frank didn't necessarily go to Gary, 'Oh my God, this is absolutely tragic. Look, what I've done,'" Simon said. "Because he thought he'd repaired what he thought was a relatively small graze. Obviously not, because it sounds like the group behind thought it was more than that."

Frank said in his first statement that he "should have called a referee to check the repair, and I've learned from that." Instead, he continued on with his round, double-bogeying the ensuing hole — the par-4 12th — before birdieing three of the final five holes to sign for a three-under 69 that moved him to 15 under for the week, well inside the then-12-under cutline.

After Frank and his playing partners had signed their cards, Simon said they were summoned into a room in the clubhouse and asked about what had happened on the 11th green. Simon said one of the two players — he declined to identify which one — said, "'Frank, on the 11th green, accidentally took a graze out of the green. We did our best to repair it and move on.' Then they asked the other two [players] to leave and told Frank he was being disqualified."

Displeased with the decision, Frank appealed it on Monday night through his manager, Martin Williams. Simon said Frank received a Tuesday-morning call from the DPWT, asking if he could promptly convene with tour officials at Infinitum, the Q-school venue. "So Frank quickly got dressed, drove in, went to the referee's hut and had the meeting in there," Simon said.

The DPWT said it does not comment on ruling appeals, but GOLF has learned that the tournament committee did, for a second time, interview Frank about the incident and also re-reviewed photographic evidence, the timeline of events and statements from players present at the incident, in addition to conducting additional interviews with the same players.

"There's various things flying around that don't seem to be too accurate," Simon said. "I wasn't there so, trust me, as you would expect, I've asked a lot of questions, because I want as much clarity as possible in this."

The rule that gives committees license to DQ players for misbehavior falls under "1.2 Standards of Player Conduct," which states, among other things, that "all players are expected to play in the spirit of the game by taking good care of the course — for example, by replacing divots, smoothing bunker, repairing ball-marks, and not causing unnecessary damage to the course."

Inside the new rules policing player behavior at PGA Championship

The rule book continues: "There is no penalty under the Rules for failing to act in this way, except that the Committee may disqualify a player for acting contrary to the spirit of the game if it finds that the player has committed serious misconduct.

"'Serious misconduct' is player behavior that is so far removed from what is expected in golf that the most severe sanction of removing a player from the competition is justified."

Nowhere in the rule is there any mention of intent, meaning one of the Kennedys' sticking points — that Frank did not deliberately damage the green — was, presumably, moot in the decision. Driving the ruling was the extent of the damage itself, as the DPWT spelled out in its only official comment on the matter, which came by way of a Tuesday statement:

Following a request from Frank Kennedy and his team to review the evidence and the decision to disqualify him from the DP World Tour Qualifying School Final Stage, a review has been completed. The ruling to disqualify him under Rule 1.2a of the Rules of Golf — made by the Qualifying School Rules Committee for serious misconduct for damaging the 11th green during the fourth round — stands.

Subjective call? Undoubtedly. If Frank had gouged the green after accidentally dropping his putter in a post-birdie-club-flip celebration would he have been DQ'ed? Unlikely. If he had accidentally damaged not a green but a fairway by angrily swiping at the ground would he have been DQ'ed? Also unlikely. Sergio Garcia, who the DPWT disqualified from the 2019 Saudi International for intentionally damaging greens, avoided the same punishment for intentionally damaging a tee box at the Masters earlier this year. Augusta National issued Garcia a Code-of-Conduct warning but stopped short of showing him the door.

"Talking about this, it's almost a little bit like brain surgery," Simon said of the nuance in the rules. "It requires careful navigation." Of the rules, he added, "They are a little bit ambiguous."

Frank has been playing steadily in recent months with seven consecutive made-cuts on the HotelPlanner Tour. That solid form continued into Q-school where he breezed through the second stage and through four rounds of the third and final stage was tantalizingly close to securing a DPWT card. Then his quest came crashing down.

"He's not great," Simon said of Frank's emotional state in the wake of the DQ. "His first experience of a little bit of viral media, and he's getting 10,000 comments all saying, 'You little b—–.' 'You dig a big trench near the hole.' 'You are Sergio Garcia.' Et cetera, et cetera. It's pretty tough."

Simon said his son's immediate next step is to hop a Wednesday-morning flight from Spain back to Florida. In the coming weeks, they'll start looking ahead to next year. Will Frank return to the HotelPlanner Tour in '27? "Maybe," Simon said. "We'll review that."

Frank's DQ has generated so much golf-world buzz that even world No. 2 Rory McIlroy was asked about it. "It seems harsh but, at the same time, I think it's good that golf and the authorities are cracking down on things like this," McIlroy said Tuesday from the DP World Tour Championship in Dubai. McIlroy added that playing golf at such a high level isn't always easy but with experience comes equanimity. "I think nearly 20 years into a career," he said, "you learn to take the rough with the smooth and know that there's going to be highs and lows, and as long as you expect those things to happen, then you can deal with them the right way."

Simon said Frank has accepted the ruling and will own it and learn from it.

"The only thing he wanted to clarify is the fact that there was no intent," Simon said. "Is he sorry? One-hundred percent he's sorry. Does he wish it hadn't happened? Yes, 100 percent. But was it an intentional act to damage the golf green? One-hundred percent no, it wasn't. And then it's up to reasonable people — do they believe that it was a legitimate accident, be that it deserves a penalty or not? Personally, I believe him."

The post That 21-year-old who was DQ'ed from Q-school? His father has thoughts appeared first on Golf.

#2856. Alibaba announces plans to list T-Head, its Nvidia chip rival

Chinese tech and e-commerce giant Alibaba announced late Tuesday that the Hong Kong Stock Exchange will allow the company to spin off its semiconductor subsidiary T-Head.

To do so, Alibaba said it plans a public offering for shares of T-Head in Hong Kong, subject to approval from the exchange and the China Securities Regulatory Commission.

The filing did not disclose a date or proposed size of a listing.

Alibaba noted it had already filed confidentially on Sept. 11 to list T-Head on the Hong Kong Stock Exchange.

Meanwhile, Hong Kong-listed shares of Alibaba traded 1% lower Wednesday morning. The stock is down about 29% year-to-date.

In late September, Alibaba announced its next-generation AI chip, the Zhenwu V900, that it said delivers three times the performance of its predecessor, the Zhenwu M890 released in May.

Alibaba is one of several Chinese companies, including Huawei, developing homegrown semiconductors to power artificial intelligence, amid growing U.S. restrictions on China's ability to access the chips.

Short seller Culper Research alleged in May that Alibaba was able to obtain restricted Nvidia chips by indirectly financing entities in Southeast Asia. Alibaba did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

News of Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's affair with an alleged Chinese spy involved with one of those entities has dragged down the stock to its lowest valuation since late March, weighing on the overall U.S. stock market.

Amid the tech stock volatility, U.S.-based AI giant Anthropic said in an updated filing Tuesday that it will offer 825 million shares in its upcoming IPO at a price range between $70 and $78 per share.

Trading on the Nasdaq is expected to begin on Nov. 19, people familiar with the details told CNBC. They were granted anonymity because they were not authorized to speak publicly.

Chinese tech stocks have underperformed their U.S. peers, despite investor excitement last year around AI breakthroughs by DeepSeek.

Baidu has reportedly delayed the listing of its AI chip entity Kunlunxin, as has Chinese memory chip company YMTC, according to news reports this month. Neither company immediately responded to requests for comment.

In the last few months, China's financial regulators have increased their scrutiny on tech IPOs and the rush of capital into the sector.

—CNBC's Fred Imbert, Ashley Capoot and Leslie Picker contributed to this report

#2855. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

Catellier ranks No. 667 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 9.60%. See Williams Technical Analysis on TipRanks.

#2854. LeBron Log No. 4: A courtside sip, 16,000 makes and Cleveland's welcome

LeBron Log No. 4: A courtside sip, 16,000 makes and Cleveland's welcome originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

LeBron James' latest week with the Sixers included a sip of a stranger's wine, yet another number no NBA player had reached and a return to the city where his career began.

It also included his worst performance with Philadelphia, followed less than 48 hours later by his highest-scoring game with the team.

Welcome to the fourth edition of the LeBron Log.

No chronicle of James' first season in Philadelphia could omit the time he helped himself to a fan's wine during a game.

James chased Tyrese Maxey's transition pass into the stands during the third quarter of the Sixers' Nov. 2 win over the Bulls. He collided with Philadelphia native Troy Clemons, spilled some of Clemons' wine and sampled what had landed on his arm.

The interaction received its own story on our site, and deservedly so. It also belongs in the Log.

Clemons did not keep the cup as a souvenir. He threw it away and got another drink.

James returned to the court and later broke into a dance after scoring over the 6-foot-1 Tre Jones. The wine clip generated millions of views across social media and became the defining image from an otherwise ordinary 19-point Sixers victory.

The following night in Detroit, James offered a broader explanation for why Philadelphia has already looked comfortable together.

"We've got a good team," James said. "Guys vibe with one another. Guys like each other. Guys enjoy being around each other. That has a lot to do with it, too. It makes the game a lot easier when you enjoy being around people."

Eleven games into his Sixers career, James appears to be doing exactly that.

James arrived in Detroit two baskets away from another unprecedented number.

His second field goal against the Pistons made him the first player in NBA history to reach 16,000 regular-season field goals.

James finished the Sixers' 112-106 victory with 13 points, 10 rebounds and eight assists. The near-triple-double helped Philadelphia improve to 8-1 and avenge its only loss of the season.

Kareem Abdul-Jabbar ranks second in league history with 15,837 made field goals. Karl Malone is a distant third at 13,528. James is the only active player with more than 12,000.

His scoring record has long been secure. James passed Abdul-Jabbar in career points in February 2023 and later overtook him in made baskets. The round number in Detroit still illustrated the distance he has created between himself and every other scorer.

James began his career by making 5,361 field goals across seven seasons with Cleveland. He added 3,510 in Miami, returned to make 3,691 more for the Cavaliers and made another 3,421 during eight years with the Lakers.

Basket No. 16,000 belonged to Philadelphia.

The Sixers' 8-1 start also carried a notable James connection. Each of his previous four teams to begin a season at least 7-1 reached the NBA Finals.

James was not ready to entertain that conversation in early November.

"It's too early to be talking about the Finals and things of that nature," he said. "We've got to continue to buy into what we want to do."

Two nights later, James returned to the place where nearly half of those 16,000 baskets were made.

His first game in Cleveland as a Sixer brought out jerseys from every stop of his career. Cavaliers colors filled Rocket Arena alongside Heat black, Lakers purple and the Sixers' throwback look.

James received a warm ovation when he was introduced last among Philadelphia's starters. When he checked out late in the fourth quarter, with the result decided, the crowd stood for him again.

James has experienced both ends of Cleveland's emotional range.

He returned as a member of the Heat on Dec. 2, 2010, to one of the most hostile receptions in NBA history. Six years later, he delivered the Cavaliers their first championship and ended Cleveland's 52-year major professional sports title drought.

"I've seen the depths of hell when it comes to a reception here, all the way to the heavens above," James said. "Anything in between is easy money."

The reception fell somewhere in the middle. His performance landed much closer to the bottom.

James finished with eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, three assists and six turnovers in the Sixers' 119-95 loss. It was his first single-digit performance in Cleveland since he scored three points against the Rockets on Dec. 30, 2004.

He had scored at least 10 points in 396 consecutive regular-season games in the city. The run covered his first stint with the Cavaliers, every visit as an opponent and his entire second tenure with the organization.

It ended during his first trip back in a Sixers uniform.

"I definitely did not feel in a good rhythm offensively," James said. "A couple of them were careless. A couple of them were just trying to make the right play and not being successful with it."

It was only the 10th single-digit regular-season game of James' 1,630-game career. He still took time afterward to consider what an eventual jersey retirement in Cleveland could represent.

"The journey from being an 18-year-old rookie, to leading a franchise to a championship," James said. "And everything in between."

James did not carry the rough night into Chicago.

Less than 48 hours after struggling in Cleveland, he scored a season-high 22 points in the Sixers' 127-121 victory over the Bulls. Thirteen came during the second quarter, when James helped Philadelphia turn an early deficit into a halftime lead.

His first basket produced another milestone.

James entered Sunday trailing Abdul-Jabbar's 1,895 points for the most scored against the Bulls in NBA history. His first-quarter three-pointer moved him into the lead. He ended the afternoon with 1,916.

The most interesting play from his night was a pass.

With the Sixers ahead by four and 2:20 remaining, James drove into the paint. Three Chicago defenders collapsed toward him. Maxey stood between the left wing and corner, but even he did not believe James could see him.

James fired the ball through the defense. Maxey sidestepped Isaac Okoro and made the three-pointer, pushing Philadelphia's lead to seven.

After his postgame interview, James pulled out his phone and watched the play again.

"He didn't think I saw him," James called out from his locker as reporters asked Maxey about the possession.

Maxey acknowledged that he had positioned himself to rebound because he expected James to shoot.

"Honestly, I was like this," Maxey said, raising his hands. "I thought he was about to shoot a layup."

James has spent the opening weeks of the season teaching his new teammates to expect the unexpected pass.

"You've got to become a wide receiver or a catcher at that point," Maxey said. "You never know when you're going to get the ball.

#2853. Bessent reportedly moved to military base over Iranian threats on his life

U.S. Treasury Secretary Scott Bessent was reportedly moved from his Washington, D.C., home to a military base due to Iranian threats on his life.

The New York Times, citing officials familiar with the matter, said that U.S. officials had concluded Iranian plotting against current and former American officials had reached a more advanced stage.

Separately, Reuters reported that the severity of the threats against Bessent was not immediately clear.

Bessent has led "Operation Economic Outcast," a Treasury campaign launched in August to isolate Iran economically through sanctions and other financial measures, in addition to the U.S. military actions.

The Times reported that Bessent's role in that campaign has made him one of the Trump administration officials Iran most wanted to target.

The move comes as the Department of Justice Department said late Tuesday that three Iranian nationals had been indicted on terrorism-related charges.

The department said the three were allegedly involved in nearly 20 attacks and attempted attacks across Europe and the United States.

The alleged plots included a September 2026 attack targeting RAF Fairford in the U.K., where U.S. military personnel were stationed.

"Anyone who plans a terrorist plot on a military base housing American servicemembers will be prosecuted by the United States government," Attorney General Todd Blanche said.

Earlier in October, British Prime Minister Andy Burnham said Iran was involved in the incident at RAF Fairford that led to the arrest of five men on suspicion of explosives and terrorism offences.

Iranian Foreign Minister Abbas Araghchi then rejected the allegation, writing on X that Burnham was "barking up the wrong tree."

RAF Fairford, in Gloucestershire, is a key U.S. Air Force operating location in Europe and has supported deployments of American strategic bombers and other aircrafts.

Separately, U.S. Secretary of State Marco Rubio reportedly said military action against Iran may not be necessary if economic sanctions and Washington's naval blockade are working.

On Monday, President Donald Trump said on Truth Social that "Qatar, Saudi Arabia, UAE, Bahrain, Kuwait, and others, would prefer the status quo, in other words, a continuation of the Greatest Naval Blockade in History."

"We are seeing positive momentum toward an end to this Conflict," Trump added.

Asked Tuesday why he would not simply declare an end to the Iran war, Trump said he wanted to ensure U.S. allies in the region were "not hurt" and that there was a "long term solution.

However, Iranian officials have denied that negotiations are underway. Security chief Mohsen Rezaei said claims that talks were continuing were aimed at "easing economic pressures and reducing oil prices."

Brent and West Texas Intermediate crude prices edged higher during Wednesday's Asia trading session, at about $92 and $86 per barrel, respectively.

#2852. Chargers fail to make big moves at trade deadline despite abysmal season

Coach Jim Harbaugh and the Los Angeles Chargers haven't found much success during the 2026 NFL season.

The Chargers finished both the 2024 and 2025 seasons at 11-6, raising expectations that the team would be back in the playoff mix. Instead, the Chargers have lost eight straight games to start the season.

General manager Joe Hortiz and the front office decided not to be an overly active participant in the trade deadline, even with trade assets on the roster such as quarterback Justin Herbert, outside linebacker Khalil Mack and receiver Ladd McConkey.

ANALYSIS: NFL trade deadline winners, losers

While there were expectations for the Chargers to be a potential seller at the trade deadline, the team remained quiet.

If there was a chance for them to be buyers, it could have included them trying to help the offense.

Harbaugh stated during his press conference on Nov. 9 that Hortiz would "be open and active in pursuing" options that would make the team better.

In the hours leading up to the deadline, only one move was made and it was geared toward helping the franchise in the future.

The Chargers traded linebacker Daiyan Henley to the Denver Broncos for a pair of draft picks. The Chargers now hold the rights to the Broncos' fourth-round pick in the 2027 draft and fifth-round pick in 2028.

Henley was in the fourth year of his four-year rookie contract and is eligible to become a free agent ahead of the 2027 season.

If Los Angeles was unable to come to terms on a potential extension, moving on from him during a lost season and acquiring draft assets serves as a good move by the organization.

USA TODAY Sports' Jack McKessy writes: "The fourth-year linebacker's contract expires after this season, and he has struggled to adjust to new defensive coordinator Chris O'Leary's system since Jesse Minter departed for the Ravens' head coaching job."

More: Winless Chargers make first move of NFL trade deadline with AFC West partner

With Henley no longer on the roster, the Chargers move on with the next man up. Troy Dye and Marlowe Wax will be tasked with filling that void.

The depth chart reveals that there's still a lack of depth among the inside linebackers group for the rest of the season.

#2851. Paul George Outplays Jaylen Brown in First Battle of Celtics-76ers Trade Centerpieces

Paul George Outplays Jaylen Brown in First Battle of Celtics-76ers Trade Centerpieces originally appeared on NESN. Add NESN as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Plenty of basketball fans were excited to watch the individual battle between Jayson Tatum and Jaylen Brown on Tuesday, but another star forward stole the show.

Paul George scored a game-high 36 points on 14-of-22 shooting from the field in the Celtics' 123-101 win over the 76ers, helping his team jump to the top of the Eastern Conference standings.

George, who landed in Boston as part of the offseason trade that sent Brown to Philadelphia, had 14 of his 36 points in the fourth quarter, and the Celtics outscored the Sixers 32-19 in the final frame.

"What I've learned is, I'm a little bit better with more usage," George said during an appearance on NBC's postgame show. "That's not a knock to my opportunities here in Philly. Quite frankly, I wasn't healthy [enough] to take on the workload.

"Here in Boston, they gave me an opportunity to come out and play and be myself. Joe [Mazzulla] has integrated me right into the offense and allowed me to just be myself."

That performance continued an impressive start for George, who noted that his body is "holding up wonderfully" after dealing with multiple injuries in his two seasons with the 76ers. Heading into the rivalry matchup, he was averaging 20.4 points, 5.2 rebounds, 4.1 assists and 2.3 steals per game while shooting 42.5 percent from 3-point range.

"The beauty of this offense is the game is just gonna find me," George said. "That's one thing Joe preaches within our offense, just let it find you. I'm not pressing. I'm not rushing. I'm not forcing. I've always played that game, and my best nights have come from me just letting the game happen. That's when I'm at my best."

Brown, on the other hand, had a night to forget. He wasn't able to get revenge against his former squad, scoring just 13 points in 26 minutes. Foul trouble limited his impact, as he picked up two fouls in the first quarter.

"Just got those two early fouls that kind of distorted my rhythm throughout the whole game and kind of struggled to get it back," Brown said (via The Philadelphia Inquirer's Chris Hine). "We had opportunities to get back into the game. It was a tie game at one point. They shot the hell out of the ball, executed well. We gotta get better."

The 2024 Finals MVP finished with a plus-minus of minus-27, the worst mark of any player on either team. George had a plus-minus of plus-17.

Of course, it's a little early to declare that Celtics president of basketball operations Brad Stevens has already been vindicated for his bold move. For one night, though, George showed exactly why Boston was willing to pull the trigger on such a controversial deal.

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang walked into the chipmaker's Santa Clara, California, headquarters on Monday and conducted his regular morning meeting with top-level executives, people familiar with the meeting told CNBC.

On Sunday, Huang had disclosed an extramarital affair to employees.

Huang kicked off the closed-door gathering by addressing his infidelity and sharing how sorry he was to his staff, according to the people, who asked not to be named because they were not authorized to speak publicly. They said Huang didn't take any questions and quickly pivoted to business and top agenda items of the day.

The CEO was also in the office on Tuesday, conducting meetings just one week ahead of the company's quarterly earnings report.

Nvidia did not immediately respond to a request for comment.

People inside the artificial intelligence chip giant said Huang tried to project "business as usual," even as the probe moved forward into his affair with an alleged Chinese spy. The CEO was in a romantic relationship with Alice Huang, a former Megaspeed executive, according to The Financial Times.

Sentiment toward Jensen Huang shifted on Sunday after the 63-year-old revealed in an email to employees that he had an affair, though he did not disclose with whom he had the relationship.

"I know an apology cannot undo what I have done," he wrote. "I hope our family will heal and that, over time, I can earn back your trust." Nvidia's board opened an investigation and hired Wachtell, Lipton, Rosen & Katz to conduct the probe on Tuesday.

Employees inside the firm described the mood to CNBC as "sad." One employee told CNBC that they were shocked by Jensen Huang's revelations.

People also told CNBC that longtime employees viewed Jensen Huang as an idol who could do no wrong and thought of him as a guiding light. Many employees at the company are also struggling to come to grips with the CEO's actions, the people said.

Jensen Huang co-founded Nvidia in 1993 to build graphics processing units for video game graphics. In recent years, the company has become synonymous with the AI boom, powering the technology that underpins large language models and the massive data center buildout.

A central figure in the development of every AI layer, the Nvidia CEO is also a key voice in Washington, even accompanying President Donald Trump on his trip to China in May. In September, Jensen Huang and his wife were pictured at the same table as Chinese President Xi Jinping for the White House's state dinner.

The news of the CEO's affair has drawn increased scrutiny on his continued support for more AI chip sales to China.

#2846. Observations after Sixers lose 1st Brown vs. Tatum matchup, George scores 36

Observations after Sixers lose 1st Brown vs. Tatum matchup, George scores 36 originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Round 1 of the new-look Sixers vs. the new-look Celtics decisively went Boston's way on Tuesday night.

In the first meeting between the teams since the July trade that sent Jaylen Brown to Philadelphia and Paul George to Boston, the Celtics earned a 123-101 win at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

The Sixers dropped to 9-3 and the Celtics improved to 8-3.

George was the game's brightest star, scoring 36 points on 14-for-22 shooting. Jayson Tatum added 21 points.

Tyrese Maxey's 24 points led the Sixers. Brown was hampered by foul trouble in his heavily anticipated first outing against Tatum. He posted 13 points, five rebounds and two assists in 26 minutes.

The Sixers will host the Raptors on Thursday night. Here are observations on their blowout loss to the Celtics:

To no one's surprise, Brown and Tatum guarded each other to open the night.

VJ Edgecombe defended a former teammate in George, who scored Boston's first four points with a pair of pure jumpers. On the other end, Maxey made the Sixers' first three field goals.

Judging by volume, the home fans cared most about the much-scrutinized Brown vs. Tatum matchup. The crowd was excited early when Brown snagged a steal and then dished to Maxey for a fast-break three-pointer. The fans also dialed up the noise when Brown sized up Tatum in isolation. He wound up dribbling the ball off his foot and out of bounds. Tatum pointed to signal it was Celtics ball.

Boston's star forward had a productive first quarter, posting 11 points. He scored an easy two when he used a back screen to break free from Brown and convert a layup that put the Celtics up 11-7. Boston began 9 for 12 from the floor as a team.

As for Brown, he sat shortly after being called for his second foul with 6:48 left in the first quarter.

Brown re-entered at the start of the second quarter. The Sixers still struggled to eat into Boston's lead.

A Sam Hauser triple extended the Celtics' advantage to 12 points. There was some audible frustration in the building after Brown missed two free throws on the next possesion.

The Sixers' deficit hit 16 points on a Hugo Gonzalez jumper. Meanwhile, the Sixers missed 12 of their first 14 three-point tries. While some of those shots were open looks that simply didn't go down, the Sixers also rushed up several contested jumpers before they made Boston's defense do serious work.

In the bigger picture, it wouldn't be remotely surprising if shooting stands out as a weak area for the Sixers come the trade deadline. They entered the game ranked 27th in the NBA in three-point frequency outside of garbage time and 22nd in three-point accuracy, according to Cleaning the Glass.

Joel Embiid (16 points, six rebounds) had an injury scare late in the second quarter when he fell hard after missing a layup in transition. He rose gingerly and limped through the final 30 seconds or so of the first half.

The Sixers' star big man was fine to start the third quarter and even drained a long-overdue three on the opening possession. He'd been in a terrible outside shooting slump (3 for 32 from deep on the season) before that shot.

Brown's foul trouble persisted. He picked up his fourth personal with 10:40 to go in the third quarter.

The Sixers stormed back without him and their defense played a large role. After LeBron James rumbled in for a layup, Dean Wade stonewalled Payton Pritchard on the perimeter to force a shot-clock violation. Wade later canned a three to even the contest at 76-all.

The Celtics responded and Tatum led the charge. His third-quarter exploits included a highlight slam on James and a top-of-the-key three to build Boston's lead back up to nine points.

Pritchard buried a three on the first possession of the fourth quarter and the Celtics' advantage kept growing. George celebrated a triple that stretched his team's lead to 100-82. The Sixers' spirits were considerably lower as they headed to their bench for a timeout. As a Sixer, George only had one higher-scoring game (39 points) than his performance Tuesday.

George and Tatum were each excellent in Philadelphia. Outside of Maxey, who finished 8 for 12 from the field, the Sixers didn't receive similar games from their stars.

Brown will be back in Boston on Jan. 21 when the Sixers visit the Celtics. He'll try to even the score.

#2844. Millions of dollars poured into prediction markets on election night, trading on Democratic control of Congress and Texas governor's race

More than 87% of all election markets across Kalshi and Polymarket gave the right candidate at least a 90% chance of winning their race 24 hours before election night, a CNBC analysis found.

Election night showed how traders reacted to real-time midterm results, as trading activity surged and market prices shifted to Democrats after results from Indiana began rolling in before 7 p.m. on Nov. 3. Many midterm races were uncompetitive, which boosted the overall hit rate on prediction markets.

"I'd say prediction markets did pretty well," said Koleman Strumpf, a professor of economics at Wake Forest University. "But they didn't outperform the best," he added, referring to traditional forecast polls that tracked results that night.

Odds and gambling platforms do not use methodologies used by traditional political polling, and therefore are not substitutes for political polls.

Prediction markets saw big trades on election night.

After 8 p.m. on Nov. 3, at least $1 million was traded on a Kalshi market that incumbent Gov. Greg Abbott would be reelected. Abbott eventually lost to Democrat Gina Hinojosa by more than 700,000 votes, according to the Associated Press. A little after 11 p.m. on election night, more than $1.4 million was traded on Kalshi that Republicans would not secure control of the House of Representatives.

Following the midterms, both Polymarket CEO Shayne Coplan and Kalshi CEO Tarek Mansour were on the social media platform X crediting prediction markets for correctly predicting races. Nate Silver, a political forecaster and founder of the Silver Bulletin newsletter, disputed the claim, arguing forecast models influenced prediction market movement. Silver joined Polymarket as an advisor in 2024.

"Prediction market probabilities strongly converged toward [our models] in individual Senate and gubernatorial races," Silver wrote on Silver Bulletin. "I was tracking this stuff closely, precisely because I envisioned that we might wind up in this sort of debate where prediction markets are low-key stealing valor from the models."

Prediction market volume on midterm election-related contracts across Kalshi and Polymarket more than tripled from the last week of October into the midterms week, according to a CNBC analysis of data published on Prediction.com. The data did not include combo markets, which bundle multiple contracts and pay out only if every leg resolves in the trader's favor.

Still, volume on midterm contracts pales in comparison with sports volume, which remains larger than all other categories.

Kalshi exceeded $3 billion in total midterm contract volume, according to the platform's website, which also includes volume for contracts on mayoral races. Last week, Polymarket's two exchanges reported nearly $700 million in volume for midterm contracts.

While prediction markets broadly anticipated Democratic control of Congress, the blue tsunami is expected to bite the industry in other ways.

Three days after the midterms, Sen. Adam Schiff, D-Calif, criticized the federal regulator responsible for the platforms, the Commodity Futures Trading Commission, for allowing sports contracts.

"Sports prediction markets are sports bets. By effectively requiring prediction markets to offer contracts in all 50 states, even on Tribal lands, the CFTC is once again violating state law and Tribal sovereignty," Schiff wrote on X.

Disclosure: CNBC and Kalshi have a commercial relationship that includes customer acquisition and a minority investment.

#2843. Breaking Down the Kings’ Decision to Move on from Nique Clifford

The Sacramento Kings' decision to part ways with Nique Clifford could confuse fans, especially given what they sacrificed to land him. Clifford came into the fold with high hopes as a young building block for the franchise, but it didn't work out as planned. Even though last season was his rookie campaign, Clifford failed to capitalize on the chances he was given and became an afterthought in the rotation. The Kings parted ways with Clifford for two second-round picks, and he is now on the way to OKC.

One of the major factors pushing Sacramento to cut ties with Clifford was his failure to establish himself in Doug Christie's rotation this season, despite the Kings' struggles. Although Clifford is only at the beginning of his professional basketball career, the team wanted to see improvement from a prospect who had shown promise in the past. Whether it was production, contribution, consistency, or being a solid asset when he had minutes on the court, he failed to show he would consistently bring anything useful to the squad.

One key factor indicating how much the team lost trust in Clifford is that other players were able to pass him in the rotation. To be more exact, both Daeqwon Plowden and Emmanuel Sharp became those players who got chances to play over Clifford. In addition, the emergence of Leaky Black became yet another player who moved ahead of Clifford in the Sacramento hierarchy. For a prospect expected to be among the team's younger core players, the news was hardly good.

Sacramento Kings guard Nique Clifford (10) shoots the ball against Los Angeles Clippers center Brook Lopez (11) during the third quarter at Intuit Dome. Photo Credit: Kiyoshi Mio, Imagn Images via Reuters Connect

Perhaps the hardest aspect of the entire scenario for Kings fans to deal with is Clifford's move to the Oklahoma City Thunder. The Thunder have proven themselves to be one of the best NBA franchises at nurturing young talent and finding players who fit its system. Clifford's ability to find his place on another team is therefore especially difficult for the people of Sacramento to digest. Nobody wants to see their former Kings prospect blossom into an NBA player after being traded by them.

Another reason Clifford's departure is hard to cope with is that Sacramento had to give up something substantial to draft him with the No. 24 pick in 2025. The Kings sent a 2027 first-round pick (top-16 pick via San Antonio) to Oklahoma City to land him. Future first-round picks are considered highly valuable assets, especially for an NBA team looking to build for the coming years. Fans naturally expect a longer period to evaluate such prospects and get better returns on the investment.

Overall, the Kings' decision has everything to do with striking a balance between patience and opportunity cost. While the Kings might be going through a rebuilding process, Sacramento definitely cannot afford to waste endless time on their young players as they sort themselves out. Rebuilding is about understanding who needs the chance and who doesn't belong there in the first place. Clifford only being in his second season makes this decision quite surprising, though.

But there's also the idea that the Kings have to build a culture of competitiveness rather than giving players minutes just because they're of age or draft-pick-heavy. Players are supposed to get a chance to prove themselves, but they have to show they can capitalize on that opportunity. Plowden, Sharp, and now Black have given the Kings additional options to consider, making it tougher for the team to pursue Clifford. Of course, letting go of a player drafted but holding on to him out of pure loyalty wouldn't necessarily help the team improve.

Without a doubt,  Clifford's departure from the Kings ranks as a huge letdown, considering all that was expected of him and what it cost the team to bring him to Sacramento. It became hard to keep him on the roster, given that he wasn't productive or couldn't hold a spot in the rotation.

The fact that Clifford was traded to OKC only frustrates fans further, who may wonder how he could have turned out differently in another setting. There is plenty of time for Clifford to grow into an excellent player, and the Thunder may yet make good use of his abilities.

#2840. Observations after well-balanced Sixers hold off Bulls

Observations after well-balanced Sixers hold off Bulls originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Still no losing streaks to speak of for the 2026-27 Sixers.

The Sixers earned a 127-121 win Sunday over the Bulls to wrap up a 2-1 road trip. They moved to 9-2 overall and Chicago fell to 1-8.

Every Sixers starter scored in double figures.

Three reached the 20-point mark in Jaylen Brown (29 points), Tyrese Maxey (27 points) and LeBron James (22). Joel Embiid had 18 points and 10 rebounds.

Josh Giddey posted 19 points, 15 assists and seven rebounds for the Bulls.

The Sixers were without Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Bulls' absences included Norman Powell (right shoulder inflammation/soreness) and Zach Collins (respiratory illness).

Next on the Sixers' schedule is a home game Tuesday night vs. the Celtics. Here are observations on their win over Chicago:

Following his 0-for-9 night Friday in the Sixers' loss to the Cavaliers, Embiid did not snap right back into form.

He was well short on his first shot attempt, a wide-open three-pointer. Embiid then failed to score in the post over Jalen Smith.

Embiid got a field goal to go at the 6:42 mark of the first quarter when he tipped in his own miss. However, Smith canned a three on the ensuing possession to give Chicago a 23-16 lead.

James committed two early turnovers, although he did sink a corner three-pointer during a fast-paced first quarter. VJ Edgecombe added two long-range jumpers in the first.

Entering Sunday, the Sixers' minus-9.6 net rating in the first quarter ranked 27th in the NBA. They managed to hold a one-point lead in Chicago after the first.

The Sixers' offensive rebounding was their greatest strength in the first quarter. They poured in 17 second-chance points. Embiid had four offensive boards in the opening frame.

Jabari Walker assumed backup center duties for the second straight game without Bona. He picked up where Embiid left off on the glass, grabbing seven rebounds (three offensive) in a great first stint.

Though he's undersized in the height department for an NBA center at 6-foot-7, Walker has plenty of rebounding tools.

He's listed at a strong 237 pounds, doesn't mind contact whatsoever, hunts the ball, and has a knack for winning contested boards. Walker showed all of that Sunday. Very hard to overstate how well he rebounded.

The Sixers' first-shot offense also had many bright moments in the second quarter.

James got rolling on his way to a 16-point first half, Embiid made a couple of mid-range jumpers, and Maxey hit two free throws put the Sixers up 60-50 late in the second period.

Chicago evened the game at 68-all early in the third quarter on a Matas Buzelis three, but the Bulls couldn't sustain that run.

The Bulls' defense continued to look like one of the worst in the NBA and the Sixers' well-balanced offense kept the scoreboard moving. Brown nailed a turnaround jumper to give his team a double-digit lead. Walker knocked down a top-of-the-key triple to extend the Sixers' advantage to 12 points.

The Sixers had two apparent injury scares in the second half. Brown limped to the sideline with 6:55 to go in the third quarter, but he stayed in the game. Edgecombe headed to the locker room after subbing out at the 9:08 mark of the fourth quarter. He returned to the Sixers' bench and ultimately returned for a final stint.

On the floor, the Sixers couldn't ease to victory. Bulls rookie Caleb Wilson trimmed Chicago's deficit to 116-113 by draining a three. Sixers head coach Nick Nurse called timeout.

The Sixers were efficient enough in the half court down the stretch.

Embiid made a put-back hoop and a pair of free throws. Maxey drilled a side-step three-ball after a wraparound feed from James. The Sixers converted their clutch free throws and secured a winning road trip.

#2836. A massive trade in Nvidia just went off – here are the details

The biggest options trade on Nvidia stock Monday seems to think the controversy swirling around the chip leader and its embattled CEO is creating a buying opportunity.In a trade about an hour after the opening bell that involved more than $100 million in options premium, someone exchanged an existing call spread for a more bullish position that captures the next five months of trading in Nvidia.The core position was the purchase of 52,000 195-strike calls expiring Feb. 19 worth about $57 million – contracts the trader opened for a new long position while simultaneously selling the same number of 245-strike calls for the same expiry, lowering the cost of the trade by $10 million. The net position, a bullish call spread, needs Nvidia to trade above around $206 by expiry, or about 15% higher, to be profitable. If Nvidia stock reaches the short, 245-strike call, it would represent a nearly 40% rally and return the stock to its all-time high of $243, that was reached on October 6th.The trade was likely part of a "roll" down from an existing 210/255-strike call spread worth about $42 million that was closed out, according to SpotGamma analysis.

"The person got more aggressive rolling this spread down to lower strikes to get more upside exposure by getting higher delta," SpotGamma founder Brent Kochuba said by phone. "They're trading the call spread here because the vol is expensive going into earnings. It's a significant increase in upside delta exposure."Delta is options parlance for describing how quickly an options price will react to a change in the underlying stock price. The trader's updated position, taken at lower strikes, will make money faster if Nvidia rallies. In other words, it's a higher conviction trade that the chipmaker's stock will soon rebound. The 195-strike calls they bought for $57 million was the single biggest options trade on the tape Monday, according to SpotGamma. Of the top 20 options trades by dollar-amount, 12 were Nvidia, the data show.Flows around the stock generally leaned bullish by midday, with 57% of options volume tied to calls and more than $660 million of call premium likely initiated by call-buyers, SpotGamma data show. The most popular single contract by volume was the 180-strike call expiring Monday, that needs Nvidia to recover 1.2% by the bell to break even.

#2834. Raiders trade deadline notes

Here are some notes about their deadline decisions:

The Raiders, at 4-5, didn't decide to make it a fire sale. They also didn't opt to add at the trade deadline either as they have lost five of the last six games after starting 3-0.

NFL reporter Jordan Schulz reported that the Raiders did receive recent trade calls on star defensive end Maxx Crosby, but resisted them after a nixed trade with the Baltimore Ravens in March. Because the Raiders have already agreed to trade Crosby once, expect them to receive more calls in the future, including next offseason.

Spytek said the Cousins' trade is not an indication the team is giving up on the season.

Coach Klint Kubiak said Mendoza is prepared, physically and mentally, for this task and he praised Cousin for his influence on the rookie. Spytek said Mendoza has passed every test since being drafted and he helped the Raiders make the tough decision to deal Cousins. Spytek said Mendoza's play in practice was difficult to ignore.

Spytek said he only received calls on Cousins and did not make any himself. The talks with Minnesota emerged Tuesday morning after Carson Wentz's horrible showing in a loss Monday night. Spytek said the team did not expect this to happen before Tuesday, thus he had no previous conversations with Cousins about it.

Spytek said he and Kubiak spent a lot of time before agreeing to the Cousins trade, but, ultimately decided it was best for the franchise.

#2831. Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup

Observations after Sixers suffer blowout loss to Cavs, fall in NBA Cup originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers couldn't rekindle the magic of their comeback home-opening win over the Cavaliers.

They fell Friday night in NBA Cup action, suffering a 119-95 defeat in Cleveland. The Sixers dipped to 8-2 and the Cavs improved to 5-4.

The Sixers' top scorers were Jaylen Brown (20 points), VJ Edgecombe (19 points) and Tyrese Maxey (17).

The Cavs got 23 points from Donovan Mitchell and 22 from Evan Mobley.

The Sixers' injured players included center Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain). Cleveland was down big man Jarrett Allen (right knee contusion). Sam Merrill sat out the second half with a right elbow injury.

With Friday's loss, the Sixers are 1-1 in Eastern Conference Group B of the NBA Cup. Their next Cup game is scheduled for Nov. 25 against the Heat.

The Sixers will conclude their three-road trip Sunday afternoon against the Bulls. Here are observations on their blowout loss to Cleveland:

After a missed Merrill three-pointer, the Sixers steamed ahead on the fast break and Edgecombe opened the scoring with a slam.

The Cavs replied with a 15-0 run kickstarted by six straight Mitchell points. Much like in the Sixers' Oct. 22 win over Cleveland, they were many levels below their best in the first quarter.

The Sixers began 0 for 4 from three-point range, including two LeBron James misses. The Cavs were also the better team in transition and limited the Sixers' chances to attack in the open floor by taking excellent care of the ball in the early going. Edgecombe was called for his second foul with 7:34 left in the first quarter.

Meanwhile, Joel Embiid expressed his frustration about a couple of early no-calls. Embiid's larger problem was that he couldn't hit any sort of shot besides free throws.

He went 0 for 9 from the floor in the game. The seven-time All-Star finished with six points and seven rebounds.

Embiid has had a subpar shooting start to the year, especially from three-point territory — he's now 3 for 28 (10.7 percent) — but Friday was the kind of extreme night that's bound to happen here and there over the course of a long season.

Of course, it's notable that Embiid's been playing through right foot plantar fasciitis and the Sixers have been keeping a close eye on his minutes. We'll see how the team manages his availability moving forward and whether Embiid is able to bounce back next time out.

James didn't play anywhere near his finest game either. He had eight points on 3-for-12 shooting, eight rebounds, three assists and six turnovers.

As a team, the Sixers were 36.5 percent from the field and 25 percent beyond the arc.

Jabari Walker rejoined the Sixers' rotation as the backup center with Bona sidelined. Anfernee Simons, former Cav Dean Wade and Kentavious Caldwell-Pope all remained rotation fixtures.

With Walker and Caldwell-Pope on the court next to three starters, the Sixers cut their deficit to single digits in the second quarter. Walker threw down a fast-break dunk that cut Cleveland's lead to 48-41. He gave the Sixers some good defensive possessions when switched on to James Harden and provided his usual physical, high-effort work on the glass.

Edgecombe was fantastic in the second, sinking three triples, racking up 15 points and nabbing three steals.

Many second-year players would have a tough time finding a rhythm after encountering early foul trouble, but Edgecombe was great as soon as he checked back in and delivered a ton of valuable buckets in the Sixers' comeback push.

The Sixers pulled to within 66-63 when Maxey picked a steal and threw a pretty one-handed bounce pass to Edgecombe for a dunk.

With Embiid and James offering little as scorers, Brown hit several shots that helped the Sixers stay in the game. After his 30-point performance last time out against the Pistons, Brown reached the 20-point mark for a third consecutive game.

Still, the Sixers' offense never got rolling. The Cavs maintained their lead and extended it late in the third quarter. By early in the fourth, the Sixers trailed by over 20 points.

Embiid didn't play the fourth quarter and logged 25 minutes. He's had zero made field goals on just one other occasion in his career, back in Nov. 2019 against the Raptors.

Even with their tremendous first nine games, the Sixers knew they'd hit speed bumps throughout the year. They'll try to get back on track Sunday in Chicago.

#2829. The Rams swap out another pair of receivers following latest injury

The Los Angeles Rams have placed another receiver on injured reserve this week, as Jordan Whittington will miss at least four games with a back injury. Whittington was in line to have serious reps moving forward with Puka Nacua joining Davante Adams on IR this week, but now that opportunity will go to someone else.

Perhaps Xavier Smith, who the Rams re-signed on Tuesday in a corresponding move. Smith joins Konata Mumpfield, Tutu Atwell, and C.J. Daniels in a receivers room that looks a lot different than it did a few weeks ago.

Despite rumors like that L.A. inquired about Stefon Diggs, the Rams didn't trade for any receivers at Tuesday's deadline. This is the group they are going with.

Entering the 2026 season, the Rams brought Smith back as their kick returner and punt returner before waiving him a few weeks into the year. Smith then went to the 49ers, who have since traded for Allen, and decided to move on from Smith. As the Rams enter Week 10, Smith is back in L.A.

Is Smith the player that the Rams needed to make up for the losses of Puka Nacua and Davante Adams? Right or wrong it's what the Rams just did, and for now, that's all they did to address their wide receiver room as another trade deadline comes to its end.

#2828. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2826. Celtics’ Paul George hilariously gives 76ers fans the finger after dominant win

Celtics' Paul George hilariously gives 76ers fans the finger after dominant win appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

For all the talk about the simmering beef between Jayson Tatum and Jaylen Brown, it was Paul George, the player the Boston Celtics acquired from the Philadelphia 76ers in the Brown trade, who proved to be the biggest star of the show. George finished with 36 points on 14-of-22 shooting from the field, and he hit some daggers in the fourth quarter to blow the game wide open en route to a 123-101 win over the 76ers.

In the fourth, it looks as though George was drawing inspiration from the animosity he was receiving from a few 76ers fans. In fact, during one pause in the action, the Celtics star subtly and hilariously gave a fan the middle finger just to rub it into their faces.

76ers fans are some of the most passionate in the world, and with the game getting out of hand in the fourth, getting George riled up did not help matters whatsoever.

After Payton Pritchard drained two straight threes to start the quarter to push the Celtics' lead to 15, George saw the opportunity to deliver the killing blows to the 76ers.

He pulled up for a triple to extend their lead to 18, and then he was clearly feeling himself as he drained a difficult turnaround and-one jumper and made the free throw to push their lead to 21. He would then score five more points to extend his personal run to eight, and by then, the 76ers were out of the game completely.

George is experiencing a career revival in Beantown; after his 36-point outing against his former team, he is now averaging 22.0 points, 5.2 rebounds, and 3.8 assists per contest on the year. He's also playing elite defense, and he looks like he's at least back to the form he showed during the latter part of his tenure with the Los Angeles Clippers.

Keeping him healthy is going to be key for the Celtics, as they are unstoppable when the 36-year-old forward is playing like this and even flipping opposing fans off in the process.

#2825. Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to ‘a regular game'

Back in Philadelphia with Celtics, Paul George looks forward to 'a regular game' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Paul George's presence back in town was one of the many swirling morning storylines at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

For good reason, former Celtics teammates Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum will draw immense attention Tuesday night. After all, Brown has said there "might be a little lamb chops with some gravy" between the two, if not a full-blown beef.

But George's return to Philadelphia is more than a tiny subplot. Following two seasons in Philadelphia, he was the centerpiece of the Sixers' summer trade package to acquire Brown.

George told reporters before the Celtics' shootaround that it feels like "a regular game."

"Had I been here eight, nine, 10 years, different story," he said. "I was here two years. Obviously, we didn't have the best of luck or had a great, I think, partnership in terms of my ability to stay healthy and on the floor. So that kind of played into a lot of it.

"But obviously, it's a tremendous fan base, a great sports town to play in. So that was appreciated. There's no ill will for me. It'll be fun to just compete. And outside of that, it's a normal game."

The 36-year-old George indeed had trouble staying available for sustained stretches as a Sixer. He dealt with a variety of injuries and served a 25-game suspension last season for violating the NBA's anti-drug policy.

George did play well on both ends of the floor for much of the 2026 playoffs. He helped the Sixers come back to win a seven-game first-round series against the Celtics in historic fashion. The nine-time All-Star also brought a veteran steadiness to the Sixers' locker room and was glad to mentor younger players like VJ Edgecombe.

"Just his routine … even when he got hot, he didn't try to force it," Jabari Walker said Tuesday morning. "His game was smooth and I think that's how he is as a person, just not forcing anything and having a calm energy."

George has certainly enjoyed his early days as a Celtic.

Through nine games, he's averaged 20.4 points, 5.2 rebounds, 4.1 assists and 2.3 steals while shooting 42.5 percent from three-point range. Boston will enter Tuesday night's matchup second in the Eastern Conference standings at 8-2, the Sixers first at 9-2.

"I've been kind of blown away," George said. "I think it exceeded my expectations since being in Boston. You never know how much culture is involved and how much culture changes your outlook on things and the effect it has mentally, physically, just being in an environment … and the culture that Boston presents.

"So I've been thrilled. I've been playing some of my best basketball of late, and just been enjoying grinding it out with these guys out here. It's a fun style of play and I can't talk (enough) about just the thrill, the happiness and the excitement and joy that it has brought being here."

He was asked what sort of reception he expects from Sixers fans.

"Not sure," George said. "In some ways, I helped land them a future piece for them. So with that, not sure how the reception would be."

#2823. Steelers had trade discussions about three WRs

The Pittsburgh Steelers didn't make a move ahead of the 2026 NFL trade deadline, but in news that should sound familiar to most fans, the team did appear to be doing its due diligence.

According to Steelers insider Gerry Dulac, the Steelers had "discussions about a trade" about one of three wide receivers: the Jaguars' Brian Thomas Jr., the Colts' Josh Downs, and the Titans' Calvin Ridley.

Downs, of course, was one of the players traded the day of the deadline, heading from Indianapolis to Kansas City with a seventh-round pick for fourth- and fifth-round selections, all in 2027. It's worth noting the Steelers do not have a fourth-round pick in 2027.

However, Pittsburgh could be getting a reinforcement soon in Michael Pittman Jr., who Steelers head coach Mike McCarthy said was "getting close" to a return when talking to media on Monday.

#2821. Penn State notes: Jack Ham remains in stable condition in Seattle hospital

Jack Ham, the Penn State football radio analyst and Pittsburgh Steelers legend, is making "great progress" at the University of Washington Medical Center, Nittany Lions coach Matt Campbell said Tuesday.

The 77-year-old Ham suffered a medical emergency shortly after arriving on a team bus Saturday at Husky Stadium for Penn State's Big Ten game against Washington.

"I talked to him a little bit (Monday) and talked to him a little bit today," Campbell said. "Number one, he's talking. He's in stable condition. He's still out in Washington. We got to see him after the game, which was awesome. He was doing really well.

"Again, I think that is great progress. We hopefully get him back here sooner than later."

A native of Johnstown, Ham has teamed with play-by-play man Steve Jones on Penn State's football radio broadcasts since 2000.

Ham played linebacker at Penn State from 1968-70 and helped the Lions finish 11-0 in back-to-back seasons (1968-69). He was a unanimous first-team All-American in 1970 and was inducted into the College Football Hall of Fame in 1990.

Ham went on to play 12 seasons (1971-82) for the Pittsburgh Steelers and helped them win four Super Bowl championships. He was voted to the All-Pro team nine straight years and inducted into the Pro Football Hall of Fame in 1988.

"I want to just say from my end a sincere thank you to so many people that have reached out," Campbell said. "What Jack and his family have meant to Penn State football, our program and the athletic department, we greatly appreciate the love and care that we have gotten on behalf of Jack."

Penn State still has not revealed what kind of emergency that Ham experienced.

Penn State wide receiver Koby Howard's recovery from an unspecified injury has been paused, according to Campbell.

Howard has missed the last six games because of an injury that Campbell said he suffered against Buffalo on Sept. 19.

"I think we kind of came to a decision a little bit (last) Thursday or Friday," he said. "We're kind of in a pause state right now on where Koby is at. We were trying to beat a timeline coming back.

"The reality is I've continued to say I refuse to put Koby on the field until he's 100% ready to go back on the football field. …We're in a pause state to make sure we get him fully 100% back to health."

Howard, a sophomore, caught 12 passes for 344 yards and two touchdowns in the first three games, averaging almost 28.7 yards per catch. He traveled with Penn State to Seattle for the Washington game and offered encouragement from the sideline.

"Watching how Koby has been involved, he's been an elite teammate," Campbell said. "Everybody is going to see how you led when it wasn't going well for you. It gives you the tools and that firepower when you do come back, you have the ability to use your voice in a powerful way. Koby is doing a great job."

Former safety Marcus Neal Jr. saw his first action at linebacker last week at Washington and had one solo tackle.

Neal started all 12 games last season at Iowa State and was one of three safeties to transfer to Penn State, along with Jeremiah Cooper and Jamison Patton. But he struggled and lost his starting role several weeks ago.

Campbell announced last month that he had been moved to linebacker.

"He's been taking reps at Will backer the past two or three weeks," defensive coordinator D'Anton Lynn said. "There's a lot of stuff that he does naturally well down there. He gets off blocks. He blitzes. He tackles. He plays downhill.

"There is some stuff that's different from a run-fit standpoint. It happens a lot quicker down there. The more reps he's gotten, the better he's gotten."
