#2820. ‘Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics

'Let's check the ball up': Jaylen Brown ready for first game against Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Jaylen Brown was asked Sunday whether he and Jayson Tatum had spoken since the former teammates addressed their relationship during the preseason.

Does he plan to approach Tatum before Tuesday night's game at Xfinity Mobile Arena?

"I mean, he's on his side, I'm on my side," Brown said. "We're playing basketball. Both of us are trying to win. So it's nothing to talk about. Let's check the ball up."

After months of public comments, documentaries and questions about how their partnership ended, the basketball will finally take over.

Brown will face Boston for the first time since the Celtics traded him to Philadelphia for Paul George and four future draft assets in July. He spent his first 10 NBA seasons in Boston, played nine alongside Tatum and helped the Celtics win the 2024 championship, earning Finals MVP honors.

Tuesday's 8 p.m. game on NBC will also bring together the top two teams in the Eastern Conference. The Sixers are 9-2. Boston, riding a three-game winning streak, is 8-2 and sits a half-game behind Philadelphia.

"Another good opportunity for our team to be able to show what we're made of," Brown said. "I'm looking forward to it. Big-time matchup, two heavyweights in the East. Let's get it."

Brown has not hidden his appreciation for Boston, even as more has emerged about the deterioration of his relationship with Tatum and the organization.

"I love Boston," he said Sunday. "Boston's a great city. A lot of people I developed relationships with, you know what I mean? But I'm just focused on what's in front of me right now. And that's the most important thing."

He also acknowledged everything that accompanies an abrupt end to a decade with one organization.

"Decisions were made," Brown said. "It's life. It's business. Move on. It's OK. … It's emotions. You've got to pack up and move your stuff. Your family's got to pack up and move. But it's life. It's happened to numerous amounts of people, myself included.

"So I just look forward to being able to build with my team over here, trying to make the best of my situation."

Brown faced the Celtics during the preseason, but Tuesday will be the first meeting that counts. He said he did not know whether that would introduce emotions that were absent during the exhibition schedule.

"We'll see how I feel when we get out there," Brown said. "As of now, I've got no emotion toward nothing. So I'm just ready to go."

Tatum was similarly restrained after Boston's 117-97 victory over Milwaukee on Sunday.

"It's another game on the schedule," Tatum said. "It's the Celtics versus the Sixers. In the words of Brad Stevens, they all count as one."

Tatum called the Sixers "an incredible group" with a "great coaching staff," but declined to add to the discussion surrounding his relationship with Brown.

"Drama gets clicks and views," he said. "That's not my thing." CBS Sports reported Tatum's full comments Sunday night.

Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla was even more concise.

"It'll be fun," Mazzulla said. "It'll be a good atmosphere."

Brown is not the only centerpiece of the July deal who will face his former team.

George has settled quickly into Boston's lineup, averaging 20.4 points and supplying the high-volume outside shooting the Celtics have long prioritized. He scored 27 points and made five three-pointers Sunday as Boston beat Milwaukee for its third consecutive victory.

The Celtics have not needed George to duplicate everything Brown provided. He has fit alongside Tatum, Derrick White and Boston's returning core as another willing shooter and versatile defender.

Tatum is averaging 27.1 points, six rebounds and 7.8 assists. His playmaking has been especially important for a team that replaced Brown's downhill pressure with more shooting and ball movement.

Boston enters Tuesday ranked third in offensive rating and fourth in defensive rating. It is one of two NBA teams currently inside the top five on both ends of the floor.

The Celtics have also made 15.6 three-pointers per game, the second-highest average in the league. They set an NBA Cup record by hitting 24 against Charlotte on Friday, then made 18 more Sunday in Milwaukee.

That presents a clear challenge for a Sixers defense that has generally performed well but has occasionally surrendered clean perimeter looks after committing extra help toward the ball. Tatum, George, White, Payton Pritchard and Sam Hauser can all punish late rotations.

The Sixers have reached 9-2 without depending upon the same formula every night.

Tyrese Maxey leads them at 26.9 points per game. Brown is averaging 22.3 points, 6.7 rebounds and four assists while shooting 47.7 percent from the field and 39.2 percent from three. Joel Embiid and LeBron James have supplied scoring, passing and defensive flexibility around them.

Brown has repeatedly described the team as unfinished despite its record.

"From our perspective, it's been some growing pains for sure, even though we've been able to win," he said. "The result, we're not so much fixated on the result. It's more so about the process, and we've got to improve our process and our mentality to creating a winning environment."

Boston should provide the clearest early measure of that process.

The Celtics play considerably slower than the Sixers, but their half-court execution has been more efficient. Philadelphia will want to create turnovers and run before Boston can set its defense. The Celtics will attempt to control the pace, generate three-pointers and make the Sixers execute against a defense that has yet to allow an opposing player to score 30 points this season.

There is also the unavoidable Brown matchup. Boston could use Tatum, George and White against him at different points, giving the Celtics several defenders capable of absorbing his drives without immediately sending help.

Nick Nurse expects Brown to bring something extra, even if Brown has publicly downplayed the emotional component.

"It's going to be very different for him," Nurse said. "I think probably a little bit of emotion in there, too, but also I think he's going to be very motivated to play."

Boston's rotation is healthy entering the matchup. Two-way guard Devin Carter is out because of an illness.

Dominick Barlow and Ariel Hukporti remain out for the Sixers. Adem Bona could return after missing Sunday's contest.

The betting market views the matchup as essentially a toss-up. Monday afternoon listings had the spread moving between Boston minus-1.5 and Philadelphia minus-1.5, depending on the sportsbook, with the total at 215.5.

That fits the larger picture. Philadelphia owns the East's best record. Boston has the conference's strongest point differential and has outscored opponents by 10.7 points per game.

The surrounding conversation will center on Brown and Tatum until the opening tip. Brown does not intend to contribute any ceremonial small talk.

"He's on his side," Brown said. "I'm on my side."

#2817. Payton Pritchard, Paul George preview highly anticipated Celtics-76ers matchup

The Celtics will face an old friend when they take on the 76ers on Tuesday night: Jaylen Brown, who spent a decade in Boston before being traded to Philadelphia on Tuesday.

And Payton Pritchard, one of Brown's longest-tenured teammates, looks forward to the match-up.

"I definitely had a lot of good memories with JB," he said in front of an unusually large media corp at the Xfinity Mobile Arena in Philadelphia. "Obviously, he was there from the moment I got drafted, and we had a good competitive battle. He pushed me to get better, so I'm definitely appreciative of that. And then, as far as tonight, two really great teams going at it, and there's nothing better than that."

Still, although there's heightened public interest in this match-up, the added emotions are nothing that he and the Celtics aren't used to.

"I've played a lot of playoff games, so I don't think it'll be that much different," Pritchard said. Three of those games came at the very arena where the Celtics held shootaround on Tuesday morning, just a few months ago, in the first round of the 2026 NBA Playoffs

Much of the discourse heading into Tuesday night will center on the dynamic between Brown and Jayson Tatum, whose relationship has been heavily scrutinized for years, especially in the last few weeks.

Paul George said he expects another big night from Tatum amid all the chaos.

"JT is locked in," George said. "He's as professional as they come at preparing for a game. He's been playing at an MVP level, and I think he's looking at it as another game on the schedule. Obviously, there's a lot of noise surrounding it that hopefully he's not lost in. But it's our job to support him, to help him do it, and make sure his focus is where it needs to be for us to come out here and have a chance to play against a really good team and continue to add to his MVP-performance resume this season."

Pritchard was asked at shootaround what people don't understand about NBA locker rooms, and how they differ from the locker rooms the general public is more familiar with, such as high school locker rooms.

"There's a lot of egos in it, and people need an ego to get to the NBA. And so it's definitely a balancing act," Pritchard said. "And in high school, like you said, it's all your buddies. Not to say that we're not all buddies, but everybody's got families. They're trying to make it. They got things to prove, so it's different. But in the day, I don't think there was ever beef or anything. I think it's working relationships. Some people are closer than others, but you find a way to win and keep going."

One storyline heading into Tuesday night is that Paul George will return to face the 76ers team he played for for two seasons. But there's not too much pressure on that return game for the 9-time All-Star.

"He said he's returned to four different teams before, so he's used to this," he said. "So it's nothing new to him."

George is averaging 20.4 points, 5.2 rebounds, 4.1 assists, and 2.3 steals, while shooting 42.5% from three on the season.

"He's been unbelievable," Pritchard said. "He's played great to start the year and shooting the ball unbelievable. His defense is impeccable, so it's been huge for us."

George said at the Celtics shootaround that he's thoroughly enjoyed his tenure.

"I've been kind of blown away," he said. "I think it exceeded my expectations since being in Boston. You never know how much culture is involved and how much culture changes your outlook on things, and the effect it has mentally and physically. Just being in an environment like the culture, or like Boston presents, and so I've been thrilled. I've been playing some of my best basketball late. Just been enjoying finding out with these guys here. It's a fun style of play. And again, I can't talk about just the thrill and the happiness and excitement and joy that it has brought to the end of it."

#2813. CoreWeave earnings put debt-fueled AI build-out under scrutiny

CoreWeave faces a pivotal test when it reports its third-quarter financial results after the bell on Tuesday.

The market will be closely watching what the neocloud company says about artificial intelligence spending — and how it plans to finance its infrastructure buildout. The outcome could either calm concerns over its debt or stoke more fear.

"Investors need neoclouds to quantify how much financing they expect to raise to execute on existing backlog," Wolfe Research said in a note Monday. "We think outlooks that communicate when and which avenues of funding will be tapped will increase investor confidence."

CoreWeave stock and bonds have come under pressure as fears deepened about the enormous amount of debt it has taken out to build its data centers.

Its stock is down roughly 40% over the past month. Meanwhile, the yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 15.5% on Tuesday, after jumping to 16.7% on Thursday as investors digested the election results and possible roadblocks to data center construction. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

The spread on its five-year credit default swaps — which is insurance against default and considered a barometer of perceived credit risk — is also at levels that are considered distressed. On Tuesday, its CDS spread stood around 1,152 basis points, after rising above its October peak to close at 1,278 basis points on Thursday. Rising CDS spreads mean investors view the debt as riskier.

In addition, the New Jersey-based company's leverage is high. CoreWeave has a debt-to-equity ratio of 702%, up from almost 291% a year ago.

CEO Michael Intrator has sought to quell investor fear, posting on LinkedIn after the election last week that the company doesn't anticipate "any impact to CoreWeave's existing customer commitments or associated backlog as a result of shifts in the political landscape."

The company reported a revenue backlog of $104.2 billion in the second quarter. It has about $35 billion in debt, as of June 30.

CoreWeave has already given a picture of what it expects for the third quarter.

It guided for quarterly revenue of $3.4 billion to $3.6 billion when it reported second-quarter results in August. It anticipated capital expenditures between $11.5 billion and $13.5 billion for the quarter and $35 billion to $39 billion for the full year. The company also expects 1.85 gigawatts in active power by year-end.

Wells Fargo expects the company's capex needs for the rest of the year to be largely covered by its remaining undrawn debt, debt issuances in August/September and the $12 billion it is holding for project costs, which will flow into capex, analyst Michael Turrin said in a note Sunday.

However, its 2027 capex could reach $66 billion, based on an expected capacity addition of 1.5 gigawatts, Turrin said. That will require at least $25 billion more in debt, he noted. It can be largely funded through delayed draw term loans, which allows CoreWeave to draw down funds over time, and the rest can come through bonds and convertibles, he said.

Turrin has an overweight rating and $115 price target on the stock, implying 135% upside from Monday's close.

"While neoclouds sit in the eye of the AI storm, we remain confident that CRWV is the highest quality co in the space," Turrin wrote. "Beyond the noise, we see capacity/revs coming in better than expected (1.95GW this yr vs guide 1.85GW) as margins start to inflect."

However, investors likely won't get all the answers they're seeking during CoreWeave's third-quarter results, said CreditSights analyst Jordan Chalfin.

"One of the major issues for CoreWeave is whether or not they can obtain all the financing they need," he said. "They may try and calm the markets, but I'm not sure how much color they can and will provide."

Investors will be looking for 2027 guidance on capex and free cash flow, though that may not come until Coreweave releases fourth quarter results, Chalfin notes.

CoreWeave could ease investor concerns by showing it has plans and approvals in place to scale capacity through 2027, he said. The company can also demonstrate remaining performance obligation (RPO) growth, which shows the total value of its contracted future revenue, and highlight new customer wins.

In addition, pricing has been really strong on recent deals, Chalfin pointed out. "That could show up in an operating margin beat for Q3 and expectations for Q4."

While CoreWeave faces finances challenges, the company has many ways to access capital, Chalfin said. That includes chip-backed deals, which allow Corewave to borrow money using GPUs as collateral, and original equipment manufacturer financing. For instance, CoreWeave could turn to Dell, which is a big supplier with a strong balance sheet, Chalfin noted.

"Despite all the market sentiment, fundamentals are actually quite strong and pricing is strong," he said. "The trends over the next few quarters, I think, should be supportive." Risks could arise after that, but it is "too early to call."

Some analysts remain skeptical that Coreweave will be able to calm credit fears.

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, points out that the company is facing a huge bill in just over two years. CoreWeave owes roughly $15 billion in principal payments through 2028, according to its second-quarter earnings statement.

"They don't have enough cash to service all that debt," he said. "Unless some big orders come in, unless some big money flows come in for them in the next 17, 18 months, I think this is going to be the start of the end for CoreWeave, unless somebody comes in and wants to buy them. But who wants to buy all that debt?"

However, there could be a "small" window for the company to push out its debt for another three to five years, but it will depend on how strong their cash flow is when they report on Tuesday, Lang said.

Plus, issuing equity to help fund infrastructure projects, like Meta and Oracle did recently, isn't an option for CoreWeave, he noted.

"They're going to dilute the stock so much that it's going to continue to go down," he said. "They missed that window when the stock was in the hundreds."

CoreWeave topped $183 in June 2025. It has since declined more than 73%, recently trading around $47.

Despite the fall, analysts remain bullish on the stock with an average overweight rating and $126 target, based on 39 estimates complied by FactSet.

—CNBC's Jordan Novet contributed reporting.

#2812. Raiders offer blunt reasoning to holding onto Maxx Crosby at trade deadline

The Raiders had a pretty good idea who would take Kirk Cousins' place if they traded him Tuesday. They used the first pick in the draft on Fernando Mendoza, watched him get better by the week in practice and came to the conclusion he was ready for his shot.

Now try having that same conversation about Maxx Crosby.

Who takes his place on Sunday? For that matter, who takes it next September?

Somewhere in the answers to those questions is the explanation for how the Raiders could send their starting quarterback to the Vikings and hold on to their best defensive player at the same trade deadline.

Which is why the Raiders said yes to a trade offer for Cousins, but were not interested in dealing Crosby, whom they believe is a big part of their future and an asset that can help the transition to Mendoza.

Las Vegas Raiders defensive end Maxx Crosby warms up before a game against the New England Patriots in Foxborough, Mass., Sunday, Oct. 11, 2026. IMAGN IMAGES via Reuters Connect

"We're always going to make the best decision for the Raiders," is how general manager John Spytek put it bluntly.

And it's likely part of the reason NFL teams showed little interest in trading for Crosby on Tuesday. Multiple league sources indicated there was minimal talk regarding Crosby, and indicated the Raiders had something to do with that by making it clear they wanted to keep him.

That gave potential suitors little reason to believe a phone call would get them very far.

"We say no a lot more than we say yes," Spytek said.

Crosby had been sending much the same message about wanting to remain in Las Vegas as the Raiders did about keeping him.

Even after everything that happened last March, he has continued to express his desire to spend his entire career with the Raiders. He remains under contract, is beloved by the fan base and, most important to the people making the decision, figures prominently in how they see this team coming together over the next few years.

Fernando Mendoza of the Las Vegas Raiders warms up before a game against the San Francisco 49ers in Santa Clara, Calif., Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. Getty Images

Cousins arrived in Las Vegas under an entirely different set of circumstances. His job was to give the Raiders a veteran quarterback they could win with while Mendoza learned Klint Kubiak's offense. There was always going to be an expiration date on that arrangement. The question was whether it would come sometime next spring or on a Tuesday afternoon in November.

Cousins held up his end. The Raiders won their first three games with him under center. They took the Chiefs and Bills down to the wire. And at 4-5, there was a reasonable argument for sticking with a veteran who still gave them a chance to play meaningful games in December.

Most of what he's done to make his case has happened on a practice field, out of public view. The rookie has been shining there, and the Raiders have seen enough to feel good about putting the offense in his hands. They understand there will be a learning curve. They're ready to live with it.

Kirk Cousins of the Las Vegas Raiders celebrates a touchdown against the San Francisco 49ers in Santa Clara, Calif., Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. Getty Images

And really, what more could they ask Cousins to do? He gave Mendoza nine games to watch how a veteran prepares for a defense, handles a bad series and comes back to work after a tough loss. What Mendoza needs now is the experience of throwing a costly interception himself, walking back to the sideline and figuring out how to go score on the next drive. Cousins could only take him so far with that part of the job.

For all the attention on what a contender might be able to do with him down the stretch, the Raiders are entitled to consider what they can do with him, too. They have a first-year head coach and a rookie quarterback about to make his first start. There's something to be said for having a player of Crosby's caliber around while those two find their footing, especially one who wants to be here to see where it goes.

From the day the Raiders drafted Mendoza, they could picture their next good team with him at quarterback. By Tuesday, they were ready to start finding out how soon he could get them there.

When they picture that same team on defense, they still see Crosby coming off the edge.

#2810. Tre Johnson is struggling from three. So are the Wizards

DETROIT — Tre Johnson is an elite shooter. It's why the Washington Wizards selected him No. 6 in the 2025 draft. But his jump shot hasn't clicked at the start of the 2026-27 season.

Johnson scored two points on 1-for-8 shooting in Monday's 122-111 loss to the Detroit Pistons just two days after shooting 2-for-15 in a 114-75 loss to the Oklahoma City Thunder. Johnson missed all 13 of his 3-point attempts in those contests.

He is shooting 35.1% from the field through 11 games. His 24.5% 3-point percentage is the second-worst in the NBA among players with at least 50 3-point attempts this season, trailing only Ace Bailey's 22.6%.

In Monday's loss, Johnson missed two wide-open triples — one in the far corner and another on the wing — in the first half. In the second half, he missed three more open looks to finish 0-for-6 from long range. His lone make was a 3-foot push shot that rimmed in.

Johnson is getting open looks. He's just missing them.

"[Johnson] had a great game in Atlanta the other night. This is what this guy does. I got great faith in him," Brian Keefe said. "He got some good looks tonight. He's gonna knock those down. We saw what he did last year with the percentage he shot. That'll turn. He puts in all the work, and I got great faith in him."

Johnson's struggles are concerning. But he's far from the only Wizards with an inefficient 3-ball. The Wizards made just nine of their 36 3-point attempts in Monday's loss. They're shooting 29% from three, which ranks 29th in the league.

Trae Young made just three of his 10 3-point attempts in Monday's loss. He's shooting 28.8% from three. Alex Sarr missed both of his outside jumpers to fall to 19.4% from three this season. Kyshawn George shot just 2-for-6 from long range.

Washington has a shooting problem. Keefe believes this slump will pass. But 11 games in, the Wizards' spacing concerns have been realized.

"We've got guys who can make them. I've got faith in our shooters. We're in a tough stretch shooting-wise, but these things will average out," Keefe said. " We're going to continue to do the right things, put ourselves in the position to make those shots, and I got faith in our guys."

AJ Dybantsa, who scored 21 points against Detroit but made just one of his four 3-point attempts, reiterated that sentiment. The rookie remained confident in the Wizards' ability to make open shots, especially from three.

"Keep shooting. We're NBA players. We're gonna knock shots down," Dybantsa said. "We might be in a shooting slump right now, but [we] have to keep shooting."

#2805. 30 Fantasy Basketball Takes on Every NBA Team: The rookie takeover is changing rotations

Here are five fantasy trends grabbing my attention heading into Week 4 of the fantasy basketball season, plus some rapid-fire notes on every other team.

Heat: One of the bigger surprises has been Miami's Davion Mitchell, who's ranked inside the top 55 in both 9-cat and standard points leagues, outpacing his ADP of 118.7. He's averaging 35.1 minutes and 6.2 assists per game, making his playmaking a major part of his fantasy value. With six double-doubles through 10 appearances, Mitchell has been a quiet hero early in the season.

Warriors: F Yaxel Lendeborg is another draft-day bargain, ranking 54th in 9-cat and 37th in points leagues after carrying an ADP of 104. His usage ranks fourth, but he has the highest TS% among rookies playing at least 20 minutes a night. The efficiency at high-end minutes (32.3 per game) with a defined role led to some nice cross-category value across scoring, rebounding, facilitating and defensive production. I'm buying the breakout and hard-pressed to find a reason to sell. He's that good.

Clippers: LAC is hoarding one of the best fantasy values in Kris Dunn. He's sitting at 33rd in 9-cat, despite being drafted in the late rounds (ADP 119). The steals he's piling up (over 2 per contest) are what's driving most of his rise up the ranks. He's playing a ton of minutes while keeping his turnovers down and delivering a versatile 9-4-4 line with almost 3 steals a night. There's some sell-high appeal here, especially in 9-cat leagues. In 42% of Dunn trades, he's part of a package deal, so you'll likely have to include someone else to make a swap.

Timberwolves: Let's not gloss over Minnesota's Jaden McDaniels, who is crushing his ADP (ranked 46th in 9-cat after going 89th on draft day). Through 11 games, he's on pace for career-bests in minutes, points, rebounds, assists, 3s and usage per game. I think his defense will pick up once Ant-Man returns and he carries less responsibility on the offensive end.

Kings: Sacramento's Darius Acuff Jr. has a crazy high 32.3% usage rate, eighth in the NBA among qualified players. That's a massive offensive responsibility for a rookie, and the Kings' 1-9 start gives them little reason to limit his development. His scoring (25.9 ppg) and assists (7.4 apg) make him especially appealing in points leagues. Although, even with his warts in inefficiency and turnovers, he's in the top 80 in 9-cat, well above his preseason ADP.

Bucks: In Milwaukee, Brayden Burries has overtaken Ryan Rollins in the starting lineup. Burries is averaging 13 points, 8.3 rebounds and 3.5 assists across three starts. That rebounding from a guard is particularly appealing in category leagues, and the Bucks' 1-9 start should give Burries an opportunity to establish himself. He is one of my priority adds this week.

Hawks: Atlanta has made a similar move to Milwaukee, starting rookie Kingston Flemings over CJ McCollum. Flemings is gaining valuable playing time and experience, while McCollum's fantasy outlook remains stable, operating in a timeshare and sixth man role. The Hawks have a 124 Offensive Rating with Flemings running point (small sample but a good signal nonetheless). His shot volume and efficiency have improved over that three-game span, too — now, let's see if it'll hold and if he can generate more potential assists.

Thunder: Rookie Aday Mara offers immediate appeal as a shot-blocking specialist. The rim protection, plus rebounding, give him a useful 9-cat profile. Coming off the best performance of his young career over the weekend (18 points with 8 rebounds and 3 blocks), Mara will have to earn every chance to sustain a 20-25 minute role nightly.

Hornets: Charlotte is dealing with injuries to Kon Knueppel, Brandon Miller and Dennis Schröder, creating additional opportunities throughout the rotation. Grayson Allen is the priority pickup, averaging 15-3-3 and over 3 3s per game. He's worth adding, though some of that opportunity will disappear when Charlotte gets healthier.

Pacers: In Indiana, it's Andrew Nembhard who has taken on the creator role for Tyrese Haliburton. With Hali off the floor, Nembhard's assists went up from 5.2 to 8.5 per game. Given Hali's injury history, I'd hold Nembhard even if it feels like a sell-high moment.

Raptors: Toronto's Jamal Shead has shown impressive playmaking upside with Immanuel Quickley sidelined. After dishing out 16 assists on Saturday, he followed that up with a 20-point, 11-assist performance on Monday. Keep streaming him until IQ returns.

Pistons: For Detroit, fantasy managers should add Daniss Jenkins. He started in place of Paul Reed on Monday. I'm keeping Reed even if the demotion isn't matchup-based because he can still get plenty of rebounds and stocks until Jalen Duren returns. Though, that's been delayed at least another two weeks.

Magic: At 2-7, they need Paolo Banchero back because leaning more heavily on Franz Wagner without him isn't working out. Wagner's 28.9% season usage rate reflects his importance to Orlando's offense, while Desmond Bane continues to struggle, shooting 39% from the field across eight contests.

There's a difference between a player struggling to make shots and one whose early production might be difficult to sustain.

Bulls: Chicago has the worst Defensive Rating in the league at 123.6. Josh Giddey hasn't helped, as he's been rough on both ends. Sunday was different, though — he had a season-high 19 points, seven rebounds and a season-best 15 assists in 34 minutes in a 127-121 loss to Philly. I'll be honest: I'm panicking a bit, and I'm trying to use that breakout game as trade bait.

Knicks: Another player falling short of expectations is New York's Mikal Bridges. He's averaging 6 points, 3 rebounds, 2 assists and 1.4 stocks per game. I've seen Bridges on waivers in a couple of spots and I don't blame anyone. He won't miss games, but the production has been awful.

Grizzlies: Ty Jerome is on the other side of that conversation. He's averaging 26.7 points, 4.9 assists and 2.5 3s in just 30.4 minutes while shooting 50% from the field. That's excellent production, but I'm skeptical he maintains that scoring efficiency all season. With a usage rate around 30%, I'm not selling just to sell. But if someone values Jerome as a top-25 fantasy player, I'd entertain it.

Nets: In Brooklyn, it's been a guard carousel featuring Nolan Traore and Mikel Brown Jr. It's a tough week to hold either player with just two games on Wednesday and Saturday. I'd send them to waivers for a better streaming option.

Nuggets: It's been more of the same in Denver — Nikola Jokić and Jamal Murray are first-rounders, and then there's a significant drop-off with the supporting cast. Christian Braun is the only other Nuggets player inside the top-100 in 9-cat (74th).

Rockets: Alperen Şengün is pacing to a career-high in assists per game, currently sitting at 7.8 per game, the highest mark on the team and seventh in the NBA. It's early, but Fred VanVleet's leadership is key to their success and unlocking the upside of the starting unit — just look at Jabari Smith Jr.

Celtics: Boston initially caught some heat swapping a former Finals MVP for Paul George, but it's paying off. Offensively, George is fitting right in, but he's adding value on defense, averaging 2.3 swipes per contest.

Cavaliers: In Cleveland, Peyton Watson is worth monitoring for blocks. He's blocking 1.4 per game, tied for ninth in the league with Kevin Durant.

76ers: Joel Embiid, while solid this far, admitted to dealing with a plantar fascia injury. That won't age well.

Lakers: Walker Kessler ranks seventh in 9-cat against an ADP of 40. His rebounding, blocks and field-goal percentage are driving his value, even with limited offensive usage.

Mavericks: Cooper Flagg looks every bit the part of a first-round fantasy talent. He ranks sixth in 9-cat and points leagues, which is pretty impressive for the second-year phenom.

Spurs: Wemby just missed a triple-double on Monday, posting 40 points and 11 rebounds with 9 blocks. Wemby has put up an incredible 48 blocks through the Spurs' first 11 games — landing him at No. 2 all-time since blocks began being tracked in 1983–84.

Pelicans: Zion Williamson is 27th in 9-cat against an ADP of 69. His efficiency and defensive contributions have helped offset his familiar category weaknesses. But also, sell high!

Trail Blazers: Damian Lillard is outside the top-100 in 9-cat (109th) and averaging 30.24 fantasy points per game in just under 30 minutes a night. Good buy-low spot as he's shooting 41% from the field through nine games.

Jazz: Keyonte George's ankle injury could create opportunities for Isaiah Collier. It could also provide a big boost to No. 2 overall pick Darryn Peterson's fantasy profile.

Wizards: Trae Young is pacing to career lows in field-goal (39.3%) and 3-point (28.8%) percentages. At least the assists are up to 8 per game.

#2802. How does ILB Daiyan Henley fit into Broncos’ long-term plans?

The Denver Broncos trade with the Los Angeles Chargers for inside linebacker Daiyan Henley was one that few of us were expecting with all eyes watching for running back news. But it was an underrated move that may pay big dividends down the road.

Henley is in the final year of rookie deal, so he'll clearly be looking at an extension from the Broncos now that they've spent two picks to acquire him. Where does that leave Justin Strnad and Alex Singleton after this season? From a cap savings perspective, the green dot holding veteran Singleton would be the odd man out next March. Denver would save $4M in cap with $5M in dead money. Plus Strnad is three years younger and would incur $7M in dead money and no cap savings.

The move seems to signal the teams willingness to move on from Singleton either next March or after his contract runs its course through 2027.

Additionally, Henley has worn the green dot for the Chargers the last several seasons and is the leader of that defense — one that was pretty good until the whole team became a dumpster fire this season.

Aside from cap and roster, Henley also fits well into the Broncos' long-term plans through his coverage ability. This has been an annoyingly difficult area to address and this defense's biggest weaknesses in pass defense. Coming out of college, scouting reports noted his upside and his sideline-to-sideline speed. He was considered underweight and not a great tackler. However, in the NFL that slight weight hasn't stopped him from being the Chargers' leading tackler every season.

I think this trade is exactly what the defense needs to take things to an elite level. All that remains to be seen is how well he fits into the Vance Joseph scheme this year.

#2801. Vikings bring Kirk Cousins back to Minnesota

Guess who’s back? Back again? Kirk-o’s back. Tell a friend.

The Vikings have struck a deal with the Raiders to bring quarterback Kirk Cousins back to Minnesota, according to ESPNFL.

Cousins joined the Vikings in 2018 as a free agent. He spent six years with the team before leaving as a free agent in 2024 for the Falcons.

The Raiders will receive Minnesota’s third-round pick in 2027, per the report.

It’s a small price to pay in an effort to salvage the season. Cousins has been solid for the Raiders, leading the team to a 4-5 record and throwing 20 touchdown passes and eight interceptions.

It’s the second time the Vikings have brought back a former starting quarterback. In 1967, they traded Fran Tarkenton to the Giants. In 1972, the Giants traded Tarkenton back to the Vikings.

We’ll have plenty more on this move, and what it means for both teams, throughout the day. For now, I’ll be putting on my Cousins jersey and heading down to the Olive Garden set for a reaction video.

#2799. Cy Ranch returns to Class 6A Division I state volleyball semifinals with win over Clear Springs

As Saniyah Reynolds rose above the net and hammered the final kill into the hardwood court at Delmar Fieldhouse, the celebration commenced for the Cypress Ranch volleyball team.

It had been 364 days since the Mustangs clinched their first appearance in the UIL state semifinals. Now they're starting to make it an annual tradition. Cy Ranch is headed back to the Class 6A Division I semifinals following a 25-21, 29-27, 25-17 victory against Clear Springs in the Region III final on Tuesday night.

"It's a special group," Cy Ranch coach Collea McKinney said. "I said that about last year, too, but these girls … I love our program from our girls all the way to our parents. It's definitely a community effort, and everybody coming together is what made this happen. I know we started building last year, but the girls this year got the see that, and they're like 'OK, that goal is attainable.' Now we're trying to go all the way, so I'm really excited."

HIGHLIGHT A GREAT ATHLETE: Nominate a Houston high school performer for Athlete of The Week

Cy Ranch (39-7) has won 28 consecutive matches since dropping four straight in tournament play at the end of August against some elite competition. The Mustangs suffered all seven of their losses within the first 18 matches of the season and have been lights-out since working through those growing pains. Entering Tuesday night, the six teams that had beaten the Mustangs boasted a combined .804 winning percentage. Cy Ranch has dropped just one set through the first four rounds of the playoffs, that coming during a win over Dawson in the regional semifinals.

"After last year, we were doubted a lot because we lost a lot of people," said junior outside hitter Sade Bello, a University of Kansas commit who entered the night with more than 600 kills after previously playing alongside her sister, Zora, a senior leader for last year's team. "But just knowing who we are and knowing who we have on this team, we know we can go as far as we did last year or even better if we can just stay disciplined and play our game."

Cy Ranch will play the Region IV champion, either Round Rock (35-11) or Lake Travis (41-6), at 4 p.m. Saturday back at Delmar Fieldhouse. Those teams were set to play each other later Tuesday. The Mustangs lost 2-0 against both earlier this season, but McKinney said her players are better now for some of the struggles they faced early on.

"I think a lot of growth happened during that time," McKinney said. "It was hard. The girls had to learn each other. During that time, it's pretty much a brand-new team if you look at our roster from last year to this year. They were learning how each other work, how to talk to each other and all the little things. As females, we have to learn to communicate properly in order to play together. During that time, they were going through that. I won't say we're best friends all the time, but I feel like we've learned how to play together and talk each other through those hard times. Those tough losses kind of shaped and molded them into the team they are now."

Clear Springs (38-10) saw its season come to an end after reaching a regional final for the first time in program history. After losing in the second round of the playoffs against Dawson last season, this year's Chargers were able push one victory further. Clear Springs has been a consistently strong program and has at least 29 wins in every season since 2021 under coach Shannon McClellen, who first took over the program in 2012.

The pivotal point of the match against Cy Ranch came in the second set. That set featured nine ties and saw the Mustangs stave off defeat after trailing 24-23. Cy Ranch then had four set points of its own before finally getting the final kill to win 29-27. From that point forward, the Mustangs had the momentum and closed out the match.

"Like I tell them all the time, we're better together," said McKinney, who has a 152-29 record at Cy Ranch since 2023. "We have a lot of great individuals on our team, so when we come together, I feel like we're unstoppable. And maybe I'm biased, and I should be I guess, but at the end of the day, they listened and they actually trusted each other. I think that's what made us get through that second set, because there were times in there where it got a little bleak. However, they didn't just lay down, and that's my biggest thing. We don't lay down for any loss."

Cy Ranch ultimately lost 3-0 against eventual state champion and MaxPreps national champion Byron Nelson in the semifinals last year, coming up just one victory short of advancing to Garland. This will be the second year the UIL has pre-determined sites for its state semifinal matches. Last season, Cy Ranch had to travel about four hours to Haltom City, near Fort Worth, for its matchup against Byron Nelson. This time around, the Mustangs have a significantly shorter trip back to Delmar after the realignment moved them from Region II to Region III.

Regardless of the venue, McKinney expects her team to be ready.

"No letdown ever," she said of the message to her players. "Know that you deserve to be here, and I'm going to be rooting for you the entire time. And they know that. Have each other's backs, and no letup … ever. That's our goal."

#2794. Kirk Cousins can become the first quarterback to play an 18-game regular season

With the Raiders trading him to the Vikings, Kirk Cousins has an opportunity to do something no quarterback has done before: Play 18 games in a single regular season.

Cousins has already started nine games this season for the Raiders, who have not had their bye. He’s now joining the Vikings, who have nine games left on their schedule because they have already had their bye. If Cousins plays every game the rest of the season, he’ll have played 18 regular-season games.

Since the NFL expanded the regular season to 17 games over 18 weeks in 2021, four players have played 18 games in a regular season because they moved from a team that hadn’t had its bye to a team that had: Rashid Shaheed and Dre’Mont Jones in 2025, Mike Williams in 2024 and Leonard Williams in 2023.

The NFL record for games in a season is actually 19, set by four members of the Frankford Yellow Jackets, a team that routinely played more games than other NFL teams in the 1920s. But in the modern era, 18 games has been the most a player could conceivably pay, and Cousins, as a starting quarterback, may become the most noteworthy player to do it.

#2793. Pochettino’s $10 Million Price Tag For Manchester United Would Be A Steal

Manchester United faces a simple choice: stay stuck in the relegation zone or pay the price to fix it. The 54-year-old Mauricio Pochettino has a release clause in his USMNT contract that sits at just $10 million. This figure comes from The Athletic and looks like a bargain for a club valued at $7.2 billion according to reports. Manchester United executives are reviewing Michael Carrick's future, with a meeting involving minority owner Sir Jim Ratcliffe scheduled for Wednesday. No final decision has been made, but Carrick is increasingly under pressure. If they pull the trigger, they have the cash to bring Pochettino home to Old Trafford immediately.

The cost of buying out Pochettino is low relative to the value of the job. The Athletic reports the $10 million fee covers his release, though assistant coaches cost several million more. The team's total bill would remain low despite the additional costs, and the club is the third-most valuable soccer team in the world. Real Madrid leads the pack at $9.5 billion, followed by Barcelona at $7.5 billion. United can stomach this bill without breaking their bank or needing a bailout. The financial gap between the USMNT deal and a Premier League return is massive.

Pochettino made about $7 million annually with the USMNT. United could provide him with a boatload of money on top of that via a move. One Fox Sports analyst noted the irony of the situation. "Why would he leave the U.S. for something like this? He's made a lot of money!" they said. But money changes minds. If you throw enough cash at any situation, everybody can be available. United has the funds to make this happen.

The USMNT squad is still focused on their upcoming games against Haiti. Many top players arrived in metro Atlanta on Tuesday, unaware of the rumors until they hit the news. Despite the noise, the team says they will keep working hard. "We're all very good at just focusing on the next task at hand," Robinson added.

Young stars like Cavan Sullivan are grateful for Pochettino's guidance. "Mauricio has taken me under his wing in the most tremendous way possible," Sullivan told a reporter when asked about the manager. The players love him and do not want the drama to distract them. "Everybody loves him," Sullivan said. They plan to play for him until told otherwise. This loyalty shows why a sudden exit hurts the program, even if the money is right.

United cannot wait forever to make a move. The club is staring at an unfathomable drop to the second division if they get relegated. This has not happened at Old Trafford since 1974. Pochettino has already said he wants to return to the European club game. He led Chelsea for one season and has history in England with Southampton and Tottenham. The fit is clear, and the price is right.

The USMNT faces its next test on Saturday in Miami against Haiti. The decisive second leg of the series takes place in Cincinnati on Nov. 17. The winner will advance to the Nations League final four in March. If United moves fast, they can sign Pochettino before the USMNT cycle really gets going. The next USMNT game after the Haiti series is on Dec. 13 against Jamaica.

The 49ers have been fantasy gold for receivers this decade, often overlooked because of the lack of one sticking long term. Since 2020, they rank first in fantasy points per target and still second in 2026 to the Cincinnati Bengals.

Especially when other wideouts are not available, Allen can continue to be played as a flex option, now more confidently.

#2790. Lakers ‘registered interest’ in Nique Clifford before trade to Thunder

On Sunday afternoon, the Sacramento Kings agreed to deal second-year wing Nique Clifford to the Oklahoma City Thunder in exchange for two second-round picks. Clifford held a fairly sizable role during his rookie season, when he averaged 8.6 points, 3.8 rebounds, 2.4 assists and 0.9 steals in 25.1 minutes per game.

But he'd fallen out of Sacramento's rotation to begin 2026-27 and played just 50 minutes in eight games prior to the trade. With the Thunder ravaged by early season injuries, they opted to tap into their surplus of picks and add a reinforcement to their wing room with the hopes he might break out in a more stable organization.

Oklahoma City, however, was not the lone playoff hopeful eyeing Clifford. Marc Stein and Jake Fischer reported that a trio of other suitors, including the Los Angeles Lakers, gauged the market for him. Ultimately, though, the Thunder emerged victorious in the Nique Clifford Sweepstakes.

"The Pacers, Nuggets and Lakers, sources say, were among the teams that registered trade interest in the 24-year-old," according to The Stein Line Substack, "but Oklahoma City had multiple advantages when it came to getting a deal done."

Among the "multiple advantages" referenced there is presumably Oklahoma City's wealth of picks. Between 2027 and 2033, it owns 15 second-round picks, one of which is Los Angeles' 2032 second-rounder. For comparison, the Lakers own just three over that same span.

Plus, the Lakers are also already fairly well-stocked with youthful wings worth taking a swing on this season. Adou Thiero (22 years old), Cameron Carr (22) and Ziaire Williams (25) are all 25 or younger and have garnered varying levels of rotation minutes to open the year. Given the Lakers' depleted well of draft capital, it probably didn't make much sense to fork over two of their remaining three second-round picks through 2033 for a dude who *might* crack the rotation on certain nights.

If they're going to move some of those picks, it should be for someone they know can be a difference-maker and mainstay in the rotation. Clifford, while still pretty young and far from a finished product in the league, certainly does not fit that billing.

#2786. Paul George treating return to Philly as “a regular game”

Lost in the background of the highly anticipated first matchup between Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum is a return to Philadelphia for Paul George, who spent his past two seasons as a Sixer.

Both Brown and Tatum have downplayed the narrative, as hard as it is to ignore. George, who played 89 games in a Sixers uniform, is following suit.

"It's a regular game — that's how I'm treating it," he said during Boston's morning shootaround.

George said he was unsure of the reception he'd receive in his first game back to Xfinity Mobile Arena, a place where in his last appearance, he scored a series-best 23 points on 47% efficiency to go with vintage defense, forcing four Celtics turnovers and holding shooters matched up with him to 33% (5/15 FG). It played a part in Philly completing the 14th 3-1 series comeback in NBA history.

And yet, George's brief stint in Philadelphia was defined by a core group that struggled to stay on the floor at the same time, and ran into the same second round demons they've yet to exorcise since the beginning of The Process.

"Had I been here 8, 9, 10 years it'd be a different story. I was there 2 years," he said.

It's an easy narrative to shrug off for George because, over his 17 seasons, he's gone through this same thing before.

"He said he's returned to four different teams before. There's nothing new to him," Payton Pritchard said.

George had his own contentious reunion when he played against the Indiana Pacers for the first time, followed by games against the Thunder and Clippers.

George isn't the only Celtic currently on the roster to have experienced an anticipated reunion between a foundational player and the team that drafted him.

Mike Conley, a 12-year Grizzlies mainstay before the organization ended the Grit and Grind with a full scale rebuild, was showered with appreciation and given a hero's welcome from the Memphis fanbase in his first game against them in 2019.

Their experiences couldn't have been farther apart.

In the case of George, whose seven years in Indiana ended in a trade request, he first played the Pacers in Oklahoma City, struggling with foul trouble and playing just 19 minutes in an 18-point win. When he played in Indiana for the first time later that season, he was showered with boos from the moment his name was announced, and he shot just 3-of-14 for 12 points, though a late steal and two clutch free throws contributed to a 100-95 win.

"I'm happy this circus is over with. Everybody can move on," George said after his return to Indiana.

The response was different in George's next stop; he was given a tribute video and crowd ovation playing against the Thunder for the first time. Against the Clippers, a video tribute was also presented, but there was a mix of cheers and boos, a reaction he described as "stupid," though he cited no ill will towards the franchise or his time spent in Los Angeles.

"The boos, I didn't get it," he said after his first game against the Clippers. "I still don't get it when I go [back] to Indy, but it is what it is. It's sports. I look forward to next year being back here and more boos."

It's hard to say the exact response when George's name is called in the lineup introductions tonight. As the Celtics got rolling on their shootaround inside Xfinity Mobile Arena, the song "Why Can't We Be Friends" by War blared from the speakers, a tongue and cheek choice to kick off what will be one of the most watched and debated rivalry games of the season.

Perhaps it's a sign that tempers will be calm on Tuesday night. The game will be played, hands will be shaken, and hugs will be dispersed from competitor to competitor after an early November test between two Eastern Conference contenders.

#2785. How 76ers star Jaylen Brown performed in marquee showdown vs. Celtics

How 76ers star Jaylen Brown performed in marquee showdown vs. Celtics appeared first on ClutchPoints. Add ClutchPoints as a Preferred Source by clicking here.

Jaylen Brown's first game against his former team did not go the way the Philadelphia 76ers had hoped. In a matchup circled on calendars since his offseason trade, Brown and the Sixers were blown out 123-101 by the Boston Celtics on Tuesday night.

Brown finished with 13 points on 4-of-9 shooting from the field in 25 minutes and 49 seconds of action. He also picked up four personal fouls, while former teammate Jayson Tatum outscored him with 21 points on 8-of-17 shooting.

The night started with plenty of intrigue, as Brown appeared to brush off a pregame greeting from Tatum before the two shared a brief side hug.

Watch sports LIVE with fuboTV (free trial)

Once the game began, Brown came out aggressive. He had eight points, two rebounds, two assists and two steals in the first half, going 2-of-4 from the field and 4-of-6 from the free-throw line.

Tatum had an answer for him early, though. In the first quarter, the Celtics star stayed in front of his longtime teammate and poked the ball loose to force a turnover and give Boston the ball back.

Boston took a 62-51 lead into halftime and never looked back. The Celtics outscored Philadelphia 32-19 in the fourth quarter and led by as many as 29 points. Brown added just five points after the break.

Instead, it was Paul George who stole the show. George, who was sent to Boston in the deal that landed Brown in Philadelphia, poured in 36 points on 14-of-22 shooting against his former team.

Brown had made it clear before the game that he was not interested in mending fences with Tatum. "There's nothing to talk about. He's on his side, I'm on my side," he said.

The loss dropped the Sixers to 9-3 on the season, while the Celtics improved to 9-2.

Brown will get another crack at his old team soon enough. The 76ers and Celtics meet again on Jan. 21 in Boston, which will mark Brown's return to TD Garden.

#2778. Hoffenheim claim 2-1 victory over Schalke after early Karius error

Hoffenheim returned to winning ways in the Bundesliga thanks to goals from Fisnik Asllani and Tim Lemperle providing a 2-1 win to see them head into the international break in 9th place. The match was also marked by injury concerns for Schalke, with both goalkeeper Loris Karius and Robin Gosens forced off during the contest.

Hoffenheim made the perfect start when Asllani opened the scoring after just 10 minutes. Schalke goalkeeper Karius was at fault for the goal after attempting to play the ball out from the back, only to see his pass intercepted by the alert Hoffenheim forward, who guided the ball into the net.

The visitors responded positively to falling behind, with Adil Aouchiche going close to an equaliser in the 14th minute. However, Oliver Baumann produced an excellent save to deny the Schalke attacker after Kenan Karaman had created the opportunity.

Schalke's failure to take advantage of their promising start was punished when Hoffenheim doubled their advantage in the 26th minute. Following some patient build-up play, Tim Lemperle exchanged passes with Asllani before calmly slotting the ball past Karius to make it 2-0.

Christian Ilzer's side continued to threaten before the break, with Alexander Prass firing narrowly over after being played through by Lemperle. Robin Gosens also had an opportunity at the other end, but his attempted volley failed to trouble Baumann.

The second half began with a significant setback for Schalke. In the 47th minute, Karius produced an excellent save to deny Asllani, but the goalkeeper subsequently collided with his own defender Gosens as the latter attempted to reach the rebound.

Both players required treatment and were unable to continue. Karius was replaced by Kevin Müller, while Vitalie Becker came on for Gosens in a double injury blow for Miron Muslić's side.

Schalke nevertheless continued to search for a way back into the contest. Soufiane El-Faouzi came particularly close in the 57th minute, striking the crossbar after Baumann had initially saved an effort from Becker.

Hoffenheim thought they had added a third goal shortly afterwards, but Andrej Kramarić was ruled offside. At the other end, Junior Adamu squandered a golden opportunity in the 72nd minute, firing wide from close range after his attempt was deflected by Vladimír Coufal.

Schalke eventually found a breakthrough in the 81st minute. Edin Džeko's header was initially saved by Baumann, but the rebound fell kindly for Adamu, who made no mistake from close range to reduce the deficit to 2-1.

The late goal gave Schalke renewed hope, but Hoffenheim managed to hold on during the closing stages to secure all three points.

The result gives Ilzer's team an important Bundesliga victory, while Schalke are left to reflect on a frustrating evening in Sinsheim. Despite their second-half improvement and late goal, Muslić's side were ultimately unable to recover from their costly first-half mistakes.

#2777. The Kirk Cousins contract points to a revised deal or termination after 2026

The last time the Vikings brought back a key former player for a third-round pick, the reunion didn’t last long. This time, it will last longer — but possibly not beyond the current season.

In 2010, the Vikings sent a third-round pick to the Patriots for receiver Randy Moss, whom the Vikings had traded to the Raiders in 2005. Former coach Brad Childress, calling Moss a “programmatic non-fit,” released Moss after four games.

Now, quarterback Kirk Cousins returns for a third-round pick, and a contract that covers nine regular-season games. His deal with the Raiders was structured to maximize the amount the Falcons owed Cousins for 2026 ($8.7 million, on top of the $90 million he made in 2024 and 2025) with another $10 million paid by the Raiders to Cousins in 2027, in the form of a guaranteed roster bonus not subject to offset.

The contract also includes a 2028 base salary of $78.655 million that becomes fully guaranteed on the fifth day of the 2027 league year. Coupled with a $1.345 million base salary in 2027 that becomes fully guaranteed that same day, the Vikings would be on the hook for $80 million over two years if he’s not released by then.

Without a revised deal, the current contract surely would be torn up by March. The question is whether the Vikings and Cousins would be able to come up with a new contract to replace it.

Much of that depends on how the rest of the year goes. Still, Cousins has the ability to force his way to free agency, for the fourth time in his career, by simply declining to re-do the existing deal.

However it plays out, Cousins can be counted on to make a good business decision. He has $333 million in career earnings, which has him at fourth on the all-time list.

On-field success is a different story. He has made it to the postseason three times in his career, with one playoff win.

If, as expected, the return of Cousins provides a B-12 shot to the Minnesota franchise, he has a realistic chance of getting back to the playoffs. And of winning a second postseason game.

And maybe a third. Hell, maybe even a fourth.

#2776. Raiders rookie QB Fernando Mendoza will make first career start vs. Seahawks after Kirk Cousins trade: 'He's prepared'

Fernando Mendoza, a reigning college national champion, will face the defending Super Bowl champions in his first career NFL start.

Mendoza, who led Indiana on a storybook run to College Football Playoff glory, will get the nod for the Las Vegas Raiders on Sunday at Allegiant Stadium against the Seattle Seahawks, general manager John Spytek and head coach Klint Kubiak confirmed Tuesday.

Mendoza, the No. 1 overall draft pick in April, is up for a 4-5 Raiders team that traded veteran Kirk Cousins to the Minnesota Vikings for a 2027 third-round pick hours before Tuesday's trade deadline. That deal effectively marked the beginning of the Mendoza era in Vegas.

"This is something that he's worked very hard for since the moment he walked into this building," said Spytek, who emphasized in a news conference alongside Kubiak that the Cousins trade doesn't mean the Raiders are punting on the season or looking ahead to 2027.

Spytek added, regarding Mendoza: "He passed every test. I want to stop short of saying he's earned it, but he put us in a position where we had to make a really hard decision today with his growth as a person, as a teammate, as a leader, as a quarterback."

After learning under the 38-year-old Cousins before and during the season, the 23-year-old Mendoza is set for quite the three-game test in advance of the Raiders' Week 13 bye.

Not only will he have to battle the Seahawks' vaunted defense, but he'll have to go up against the Denver Broncos and Cleveland Browns the subsequent two weeks. Entering Week 10, all three of those teams rank inside the top 13 in fewest points per game allowed this season. The Broncos and Browns are tied with a handful of teams for fourth in the NFL with 26 sacks.

Mendoza has attempted only eight regular-season passes. All of those came in relief of Cousins down the stretch of a 45-17 road loss to the New England Patriots. That was part of a four-game Raiders skid that followed their head-turning 3-0 start.

In his first year at the helm, Kubiak named Cousins the team's Week 1 starter after evaluating both him and Mendoza in the preseason. He then stuck with Cousins under center despite the October losing streak.

Cousins was open throughout his stay in Las Vegas that he was well aware of the circumstances of his role and that his time as Raiders QB1 was limited. He stressed that he wanted to put the team in the best position to win games, even if that meant at some point handing the baton to Mendoza.

But Cousins played so well that he kept his job leading up to the deadline. In fact, an ESPN report surfaced Sunday morning, detailing how, at the time, the Raiders weren't interested in dealing him.

Spytek explained Tuesday that the trade really came together with the Vikings that morning.

"I never made one call on Kirk Cousins," Spytek said, when asked if the Raiders were shopping Cousins. "I got a few phone calls, but one real one this morning."

Both Spytek and Kubiak were effusive about Cousins in the fallout of the trade. Spytek even divulged that, before Las Vegas made the final call on dealing Cousins, he and Kubiak sat with the longtime signal-caller to talk him through the deal.

"We both felt like it was important that he have say in it, too," Spytek said. "Fifteen-year veteran, everything he's given to this organization, he has a family and we kind of presented him with what we had on the table, and we tried to make the best decision. I think we owed him that respect."

Kubiak noted: "It reinforced how extremely unselfish he is and just the way that he spoke to us and the maturity and the awareness of the situation — I think we all left that room on the same page, all with respect for one another."

Kirk Cousins (right) willingly adopted a mentor role with the Raiders, assisting Fernando Mendoza (left) in his development as an NFL quarterback.

Cousins turned back the clock while working with Kubiak, posting a 20:8 touchdown-to-interception ratio. His 20 TD passes are good for second most in the league and already more than he's had in a season since his 2022 campaign with the Vikings, with whom he played from 2018-2023.

Cousins turned out to be among the best NFL rentals in recent memory. The Raiders paid him a total of $650,000, per ESPN's Field Yates, and his value resulted in a third-round pick and priceless mentorship.

He appeared to forge an endearing friendship with Mendoza, who eagerly absorbed tips and tricks from Cousins.

Kubiak said Tuesday that Cousins' influence "will remain on our team throughout this season."

He added: "And it remains with Fernando."

Kubiak was asked directly Tuesday if he feels like it helped Mendoza that he wasn't thrown in from Day 1.

"I do," Kubiak said with a soft smile. "Yes, I do. I think with the right role model, that will accelerate his growth, and we had the right role model."

Las Vegas drafted Mendoza first overall in the aftermath of his prolific 2025 season with the Hoosiers. He won the Heisman Trophy, vaulted himself into the end zone en route to beating Miami in the national title game and finished the season with 48 total touchdowns, including 41 through the air.

His effervescence, often on full display in postgame interviews, made him a unique college football star. He's still that happy-go-lucky QB, at least outside the white lines.

Time will tell how Mendoza's game translates to NFL regular-season play. He completed 6 of 8 passes against the Patriots for 48 yards. In the preseason, he had mixed results, impressing in his debut against the Arizona Cardinals and then throwing a pick-6 versus the Houston Texans and another interception against the San Francisco 49ers.

Mendoza wrapped the preseason with 240 yards on 25-of-45 passing, registering a 55.6% completion rate, one touchdown pass and those two picks.

It's been months since then, however. The Cousins trade speaks to Minnesota's desperation for a quarterback solution, but it also says a lot about the Raiders' trust in Mendoza's development.

"He's prepared, he's practiced extremely well," Kubiak said, "and his work habits also allow us to make this trade because we feel that he's ready to go, and we're excited to have him take the ball for the game here on Sunday."

Kubiak also said: "We knew this day was coming. We didn't know when it was going to be, but he's continued to grow since he's been here."
